### Chapter 1: HISTORICAL BACKGROUND

Carmona, in the early 18th century, was a part of Hacienda of Biñan, but inhabited by natives of Silang. Early settlers cleared the area, built their homes and tilled the land until a community was organized. As their population grew and there came a need to establish religious services, a decree issued on March 9, 1746 by Don Pedro Calderon Enriquez, declared that area of Hacienda of Biñan, a part of the mission town of Silang and became known as Barrio Latag. Historical accounts from the Arzobispado de Manila showed that, as early as 1823, a movement to separate from their Mother Town started. It was a long struggle, a competition of strength, courage, and wit, and finally, the brave people of Latag won their coveted dream - to become an independent town. By virtue of a decree issued by Gov. Gen. Ramon Montero of the Superior Gobierno de las Islas Filipinas, Latag became a town and was named Carmona on February 20, 1857.

Having been recognized as a municipality, the people of Carmona proved their worth. They tilled the soil and made it productive. To give everybody a fair chance, the agricultural land was considered communal and divided into parcels for distribution to family heads, who shall have the right to till for three (3) years, and in case of competition, an auction for such right shall be administered, known then as “Subasta delas Tierras Communales del Pueblo de Carmona”. The Subasta or auction became a lottery or “Sorteo” in response to the growing population and competition for the right to till the communal land.

- A. THE TRANSFORMATION In the early 90’s, the Municipality of Carmona was cited to be one of the priority areas for industrial development in the province. Huge capital

outlays were then channeled to its local government for the development of industrial estates. This brought about a drastic transformation of its vast landscape into industrial parks. This rapid development and influx of manufacturing companies as locators led to profound changes in the lives of the ordinary people of Carmona.

The increase in municipal revenues and population growth led to Carmona’s reclassification as First Class Municipality in July 1996 and also categorized as a minor urban center in the province of Cavite.

BPO industries also started to take part in the continuously growing business community of Carmona. Foreign investments are still pouring in, as export-oriented industries move in high gear. Thanks to the sustained industrial peace, competitive labor force, and good governance of Carmona.

![image 31](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile31.png>)

###### MARCELINO PALMA CLARITO

###### Pre-war

Marcelino Palma Clarito, “Ninoy” to his friends, became the town mayor of Carmona during the pre-war period. Before he became a mayor, he served as “Juez de Paz” of the town. He was married to Patricia Mapanoo with whom he has an only son they named Elfredo.

|![image 41](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile41.png>)<br><br>![image 42](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile42.png>)|

|![image 43](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile43.png>)<br><br>![image 44](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile44.png>)<br><br>![image 45](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile45.png>)<br><br>MARCELO REYES Pre-war|

###### MARCELO REYES

###### Pre-war

Marcelo “Celo” Reyes was the son of Juanita Dearoz and Gavino Reyes. He was born on January 15, 1880. He was a cattle raiser before he was elected as Municipal Mayor. He held the post for more than one term. He was married to Leoncia Paular and blessed with five children – Leonides, Adoracion, Librada, Estela and Marcelo Jr.

![image 46](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile46.png>)

###### MARTIN REYES Pre-war

The son of Gavino Reyes and Juanita Dearoz, Martin Reyes fondly called by his friends and family “Ting” became the mayor of Carmona also during the pre-war times. He was married to Antonina Tolentino, a native of Biñan, Laguna with whom he had four children – Bernardino, Catalino, Paterno and Paquita.

Rolando C. Rosas was the town’s vice-mayor when the then mayor Felino Maquinay died in 1990. He automatically assumed mayorship and continued his service until 1995. Rosa, Laguna with whom he has five (5) children namely: Redentor, Rey, Diosdado, Rommel and Mutya. At present, he serves as a consultant in the local government.

###### DAHLIA A. LOYOLA 2004-2007, 2010-2019

Dr. Dahlia A. Loyola is the 8th Mayor of Carmona after the Commonwealth period. She is the 1st woman mayor, already elected to three (3) terms. Her 1st term is from 2004-2007, followed by two (2) consecutive terms from 2010 to 2019, taking her most trusted and reliable Mayor of the Town. At present, Dr. Dahlia is the Representative of the Fifth District of Cavite, making her the first woman representative of Carmona, Silang and GMA. She was born on the 26th of January, 1961 in Manila. Her parents are Engr. Epifanio R. Ambayec, a Civil Engineer, and Mrs. Milagros AlumiaAmbayec, a retired teacher. She is married to Atty. Roy M. Loyola, the former Congressional Representative of the 5th District of Cavite and presently the Municipal Mayor of Carmona. They are blessed with three children namely: Bea, Roy, Jr. and Roy III.

Dr. Dahlia studied elementary and secondary education in Colegio de Sta. Catalina de Sena, Biñan, Laguna and finished BS Zoology at the University of the Philippines, Los Baños, Laguna. She pursued Medical School at the University of Perpetual Help System Laguna and after a year-long internship at the AFP Medical Center (1986-1987), she passed the Medical Board Examination given in January 1988.

|![image 153](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile153.png>)<br><br>![image 154](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile154.png>)<br><br>![image 155](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile155.png>)<br><br>DR. DAHLIA A. LOYOLA<br><br>2004-2007, 2010-2019|
|---|

An advocate of peace, health, education, and women empowerment, she founded the Carmona Peace and Order

Foundation Inc., instituted the Association of Women for Carmona Development Inc., and spearheaded numerous activities and programs promoting the general welfare of the citizens of Carmona. Dr. Dahlia is the current President of the Cavite Mayors League and Board Director of the Philippine Red CrossCavite Chapter.

![image 156](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile156.png>)

ROY M. LOYOLA 1995-2004 / 2008-2010/<br><br>2019-Present|
| |

The Honorable Mayor Atty. Roy “Bobby” Loyola is one of the sons of Angelo Levardo Loyola, a District Supervisor, and Francisca Maulanin, a dedicated teacher. Born on February 17, 1962 in Carmona, he served as the town mayor for 12 years where under his administration Carmona transformed from agricultural community to an industrial zone hosting industrial parks and estates.

He finished his elementary and secondary education in Colegio de Sta. Catalina de Sena in Biñan, Laguna and studied college in the Philippine Christian University-Manila, completed his course, BS Business Administration. He then pursued Bachelor of Laws in the University of the East and has the distinction of being part of the 17% Bar passers out of 2,682 examinees during the 1987 Bar exams.

He is married to Dr. Dahlia Ambayec-Loyola and blessed with three children namely: Bea, Roy, Jr. and Roy III. After serving three consecutive terms from 2010-2019 as the Congressional Representative of the 5th District of Cavite, comprised of Carmona, GMA, and Silang, he is now back to serve the Carmonians as the Municipal Mayor.

### Chapter 2: PHYSICAL AND NATURAL CHARACTERISTICS

![image 170](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile170.png>)

#### A. GEOGRAPHICAL, ADMINISTRATIVE/POLITICAL PROFILE

- A.1. LOCATION Carmona is a First-Class Municipality located in the

Island of Luzon, Province of Cavite, and Region IV. This municipality is approximately 36 kilometers South of Manila and Thirty-eight (38) kilometers from the Provincial Capitol, Trece Martires City. It is bounded by the municipality of Biñan on the North and South, General Mariano Alvarez on the Southwest and Silang on the West. The geographical location of the site is about 120 3’ latitude and 141 9’ longitudes.

![image 171](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile171.png>)

###### Map 2-1: Location Map

![image 172](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile172.png>)

![image 173](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile173.png>)

- A.2. POLITICAL BOUNDARIES Carmona belongs to the 5th Congressional District of the Province of Cavite. It is bounded on the North-East, East and South by Biñan, Laguna; on

![image 177](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile177.png>)

the Northwest by General Mariano Alvarez, Cavite; and on the Southwest by the Municipality of Silang, Cavite.

![image 178](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile178.png>)

Map 2-2 : Carmona Basemap

![image 179](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile179.png>)

![image 180](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile180.png>)

![image 181](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile181.png>)

![image 182](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile182.png>)

- A.3. LAND CLASSIFICATION The Municipality of Carmona has a total land area of 3,092 hectares representing 2.18% of the total land area of Cavite. Based on the Political

Boundary Survey conducted by the DENR on 2013, the total land area of Carmona is 2,967.8 hectares which is lower than the 3,092 land area that the municipality is using on the previous years (see table below). Carmona has no unclassified lands. No declared forest, mineral and public parks. But there are heritage lands with an area of 204 hectares agri-land owned by the people of Carmona and administered by the Local Government Unit of Carmona. National Government Agencies are now using the new land area of Carmona set by the DENR as their basis for planning and budgeting. Presently, the LGUCarmona is using the old land area since the approved CLUP (2011-2022) is based on the 3,092 hectare lot but will be updated with this new CLUP (2023-onwards).

![image 183](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile183.png>)

#### B. TOPOGRAPHY

###### B.1. ELEVATION

Carmona has an elevation of about 11 to 212 meters or 36 to 695 feet above sea level. The lowest elevation is Barangay Maduya and Poblacion Areas (Barangay 1–8), while Barangay Cabilang Baybay, the northern part of Mabuhay, Milagrosa and Lantic are at a median elevation. The southern part of Milagrosa is situated at the highest altitude of the municipality, along with Barangay Bancal and the rest of Barangay Mabuhay and Lantic.

![image 187](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile187.png>)

![image 188](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile188.png>)

###### Map 2-3 : Digital Elevation Map

![image 189](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile189.png>)

###### B.2. SLOPE

Based on the Philippine Geographic Information System (GIS), there are six slope classifications. In Carmona, only four slopes exist, ranging from 0 (level to nearly level) to 30% (rolling to moderately steep). These slopes are classified to indicate the probable limits of various types of land uses. Category A (areas that are level to nearly level) comprised mostly the eastern half of Carmona. Under this category is the lowland part of Carmona, including the Barangays of Maduya, Poblacion, and Milagrosa extending up to the steep boundaries of Lantic, Mabuhay, and Cabilang Baybay. Meanwhile, the western half represents the highland portion of Carmona, which includes Barangays Lantic, Bancal, and Mabuhay.

###### Table 2-2. Slope Characteristic per Category

|CATEGORY|SLOPE PERCENTAGE|SLOPE DEGREES|CHARACTERISTICS|
|---|---|---|---|
|A|0-3%|0o– 1.72o|Level to nearly level|
|B|3-8%|1.72o– 4.57o|Gently sloping to undulating|
|C|8-18%|4.57o– 10.20o|Undulating to rolling|
|D|18-30%|10.20o– 16.70o|Rolling to moderately steep|
|E|30-50%|16.70o– 26.57o|Steep|
|F|50 & above|26.57o& ABOVE|Very Steep|

Source: Philippine GIS

#### C. SOIL

![image 209](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile209.png>)

- C.1. TYPE/CLASSIFICATION Carmona has three (3) different types of soils, namely, Carmona Clay Loam,

Table 2-3. Soil Classification by Land Area

###### SOIL AREA (Ha.) PERCENT %

Carmona Sandy Clay, and Magallanes Loam. Carmona Sandy Clay is located on the eastern side of the municipality, starting from the Poblacion. The boundary of Silang and Carmona the land is composed of the only Magallanes Loam found in the municipality, while the rest consists of the Carmona Clay Loam type of soil.

###### C.2. SOIL SUITABILITY a. Soil Suitability for Diversified Crops

Highly suitable soil for diversified crops amounted to 1,386 hectares or 46.70 percent of the total land area of Carmona. Most of these were planted with sugar cane and other non-permanent crops. Moderately suitable soil ranked second with 778 hectares or 26.20 percent, and the marginally suitable soil has an area amounting to 461 hectares or 15.52 percent. Soils not suitable contribute land area of approximately 343 hectares or 11.56 percent. However the lack of water resources are changing the suitability of agriculture in several barangays of the municipality.

- D.2. HAZARD INVENTORY The Municipality is susceptible to a number of hazards such as flooding, rain-induced landslide (RIL), ground rupture, ground shaking, liquefaction,

earthquake-induced landslide (EIL), El Nino (drought) and pest infestation (such as rice black bug). Based on present assessments from various governmental agencies, the susceptibility, magnitude, speed of onset, its frequency and the areas covered are shown in table below. Among the hazards identified, ground shaking shows the highest degree of impact covering the whole Municipality. Based on the REDAS study, Carmona is expecting an earthquake with a magnitude of 8.0 together with the surrounding provinces that will damage thousands of structures with possible of billions of economic value lost and several injured and possibly dead. This is not considering the effects of the earthquake on adjoining municipalities that has a large effect on

![image 222](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile222.png>)

Carmona due to the integration of the municipalities and cities. Variations on flooding risk by various data sources shows that only few portions of Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay will be inundated, and the municipality is generally not affected by rising sea-levels.

- c. Liquefaction The Liquefaction Hazard Map of Carmona showing Barangays 1 to 13 are likely to experience liquefaction. Barangays 1-10 and some portion of

Mabuhay, Milagrosa and Lantic have low susceptibility, while the lower part of Barangay Maduya has moderate susceptibility. Barangay 14 will not be affected due to its location and ground structure. ###### d. Flooding and Landslide

All four major river systems of Carmona drains thru only one river system in Biñan, Laguna causing backflows during heavy rains due to typhoons. Areas at Cabilang-Baybay & Southwoods, part of PTC and Goldenmile, are mostly near the river banks and areas with clogged drainage. Mines and Geosciences Bureau (MGB) identified Cabilang Baybay and Maduya as the most affected barangays during heavy rains. Nevertheless, in a worst-case scenario, thirteen (13) barangays are included in the said exposure database for low flooding, while fourteen (14) barangays will be affected by the landslide hazards.

On June 2019, the Department of Environment and Natural Resources-Mines and Geosciences Bureau (DENR-MGB), together with the Municipal Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Office (MDRRMO), conducted a Vulnerability Risk Assessment (VRA) at the areas of concern, particularly those pre-identified high-risk areas. The focus on were on areas where population meets the hazards. The Municipality of Carmona has already implemented or are currently implementing mitigation measures before the DENR-MGB VRA. Report situation to MGB concerned and the LGU.<br>➢ Observe for saturated grounds or seeps and sunken or displaced road surfaces. Report situation to MGB concerned and the LGU.<br>➢ Activate the BDRRMC in times of landslide/flooding occurrences.<br>➢ Constant communication and updates with the Brgy. ###### The MGB-VRA showed a very high landslide susceptibility located at Terra Verde Phase 2, where there is initial recorded collapse of the riprap due to heavy rains. Based on their findings, the very unstable remnants of the riprap will eventually cause massive collapse of the structures, blocking the stream beneath it, which will endanger the communities near it and downstream in the future.

![image 290](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile290.png>)

The MGB-VRA also identified another, high landslide susceptible location is also present in Barangay Mabuhay, particularly at Dowell Quarry Site Area, where 50M Collapsed Slope Protection was observed. According to the MGB-VRA, the existing structures appear to be inadequate to hold all horizontal structures/materials above them, especially during extreme and heavy rainfall.

Finally, houses built along steep slopes at Sitio Paligawang Matanda, Brgy. Lantic are also vulnerable to any major rain-induced landslides, and communities residing along riverbanks at Phase 4 Milagrosa are already exposed to flooding hazards during heavy rain events.

Other notable observations identified during the Vulnerability Risk Assessment are as follows:

- ● There are observed displacement of soils beneath the concrete floors and evident inclination of structures towards the declining part of the slopes. Cracks to the floors and houses were also observed on the houses within this portions of Canyon Ranch Estate
- ● The Front of Canyon Ranch Estate shows steep high slopes where there are already evidences of rockslides and soil erosions.

###### e. Earthquake-Induced Landslide

The Earthquake-Induced Landslide Hazard Map is generated using the Rapid Earthquake Damage Assessment System (REDAS) by calculating the factor of safety, simulated ground shaking, and the critical acceleration slope by the new mark method. Based on the result, Barangays 10, 11, 12, 13, and 14 are susceptible to earthquake-induced landslide. The highest magnitude is situated at Barangay 12-Milagrosa with 23,817 individuals, followed by 19,697 and 14,244 individuals from Barangay Lantic and Bancal.

#### E. CLIMATE DATA

- E.1. CURRENT CLIMATE Carmona is under Type I climate characterized by two (2) pronounced seasons: dry from November to April and wet during the rest of the year.

Maximum rainfall usually occurs from June to September. The average annual rainfall is 200 millimeters with a peak of 400 millimeters in August.

The annual temperature is 27.2°C. The highest temperature occurs during May, while the lowest occurs during January. The predominant wind direction comes from the Southwest (June to September) and Northwest (October to January), while from February to April, the wind direction is from the Southeast, which predominantly comes in May.

The average humidity of Carmona is 81%. This makes the municipality’s climate cooler than Metropolitan Manila, where the average humidity is higher. Rain falls throughout the year in Carmona. The most rain falls during the 31 days centered on August 5, with an average total accumulation of 11.0 inches while the least rain falls around March 21, with an average total accumulation of 1.6 inches.

- E.2. PROJECTED CLIMATE The projected changes in climate variables for the Municipality of Carmona are based on the Philippine Atmospheric Geophysical and Astronomical

Services Administration’s (PAGASA) medium-range emission climate scenario indicating a future world of very rapid economic growth, with the global population peaking in mid-century and declining thereafter and there is rapid introduction of new and more efficient technologies with energy generation balanced across all sources.

The seasonal variations shown are as follows: (a) the DJF (December, January, February or northeast monsoon locally known as “Amihan”) season; (b) the MAM (March, April, May or summer) season; (c) JJA (June, July, August or southwest monsoon locally known as “Habagat”) season; and (d) SON (September, October, November or transition from southwest to northeast monsoon) season. The observed baseline data from 1971-2000 was derived from PAGASA’s 2011 report on Climate Change in the Philippines under the UN-Philippine MDGF Project in partnership with Adaptayo.

On average, the temperature for the Municipality is expected to increase per annum by 1.025 °C from 2000 to 2020 and 1.975 °C from 2020 to 2050. Hottest average mean temperature is expected to be felt in the months of MAM while the lowest in the months of DJF. As a result, precipitation or

![image 325](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile325.png>)

rainfall is expected to decrease by 10.2mm and 4.875mm from 2000-2020 and 2020-2050, respectively. The months of DJF and MAM will receive the lowest rainfall while the months of JJA and SON will receive higher precipitation.

Number of hot days or those 24-hour periods exceeding 35°C will increase by 1,697 days from 2000 to 2020 and 2,733 days from 2020 to 2050. The Number of dry days or those 24-hour periods with precipitation lower than 2.5mm will also decrease 6,635 and 6,565 days from 2000-2020 and 2020-2050, respectively.

Despite increasing temperature and decreasing precipitations, the number of days with extreme rainfall or those with >200mm will be more frequent by both 9 days from 2000-2020 and 2020-2050.

- F. CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT This section shows the results of Climate and Disaster Risk Assessment (CDRA) for the Population, Urban Use, Critical Facilities and Lifeline Utilities

Exposure Database on various hazards, particularly flooding and landslide.

Majority of recorded events are flooding due to typhoons. Due to the devastating Typhoon Glenda in 2014 that affected a number of establishments and crops across various barangays, the Municipal Government ensure that disaster risk reduction (DRR) and climate change adaptation (CCA) measures are fully-integrated with its programs, projects and activities to reduce exposure and vulnerability of life and properties to natural hazards.

The computations of risk scores for the different systems of interests were based on the Housing and Land Use Regulatory Board (HLURB) Supplemental Guidelines, following the concept wherein risk shall be a function of likelihood of occurrence and the severity of consequence. Using the suggested severity of consequence score matrix as shown below, the severity of consequence will be determined.

- F.1. RISKS TO FLOODS Three barangays including Maduya, Cabilang Baybay and Bancal are exposed to different levels of flooding hazards. The risk levels to flooding of

these barangays were determined using the sensitivity and adaptive capacity indicators. The discussions for flood risks per exposure element are as follows:

- a. Population Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay are at risk of flooding with moderate and high susceptibility. Expected flood depth for moderate

susceptibility is less than or equal to 1m, and greater than or equal to 1m for high flooding susceptibility. Fifty-seven (57) households or 216 family members are affected by moderate flooding, while thirty-one (31) households or 139 family members are affected by high flooding with moderate risk level

![image 349](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile349.png>)

at Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay. Since two rivers, namely, Carmona River and Munting Ilog River are situated in Maduya and Cabilang Baybay, these barangays are frequently affected by flooding events.

Meanwhile, the affected population in Barangay Cabilang Baybay is composed of 118 family members or 31 households susceptible to moderate flooding, and 114 family members or 27 households susceptible to high flooding. Moreover, CDRA Results show that a total of 355 people or 88 households are projected to be affected in varying degrees depending on their identified sensitivities to flooding. Of these numbers, 161 are young and old dependents, 8 are Persons with Disabilities (PWDs), and 54 are exposed households living below the poverty threshold.

The affected barangays with moderate flooding susceptibility have moderate risk levels since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is bound to experience every 1 to 3 years, while the high flooding susceptibility occurs every 4 to 10 years.

###### b. Urban Use

- b.1. Commercial Area Based on disaster risk assessment (urban use), high and moderate flooding susceptibility are present at Barangays Maduya and Bancal,

affecting a total commercial area of 4.21 hectares with a total population of 24,795 or 7,126 households. The vulnerability of these barangays to the impact of flooding is very low. Meanwhile, the risk level is moderate since high flooding occurs every 4 to 10 years) while frequent (every 1 to 3 years) occurrence for a moderate flood. The expected flood depth at Bancal and Maduya with high flooding susceptibility is greater than or equal to 1m, while Barangay Maduya with moderate flooding susceptibility has a flood depth that is less than or equal to 1m.

- b.2. Industrial Area Industrial areas in Barangay Maduya and Bancal are exposed to moderate and high flood susceptibility with moderate-risk levels. The urban

use risk assessment shows that moderate flood will be frequently (every 1-3 years) experienced by the affected industrial area in Barangay Maduya. Meanwhile, high flood susceptibility will occur at a moderate level (every 4-10 years), particularly at Barangay Bancal and Maduya, with a total of 1.55 hectares of land affected. The expected flood depth for moderate flood susceptibility is less than or equal to 1-meter, while greater than or equal to 1-meter flood depth is present at Barangays Maduya and Bancal with high flooding susceptibility. There are 24,795 persons or 7,126

![image 353](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile353.png>)

households affected, of which 14,244 belong to Barangay Bancal, while 10,551 persons belong to Barangay Maduya. These two barangays have the most number of industrial subdivisions where some families reside.

- b.3 Residential Two barangays (Maduya and Cabilang Baybay) are susceptible to moderate flooding that has a frequent (every 1 to 3 years) likelihood of

occurrence, and high flooding every 4 to 10 years, affecting a total residential area of 9.96 hectares. Barangays with moderate flooding susceptibility have less than or equal to 1m flood depth, while barangays with high flood susceptibility have greater than or equal to 1m flood depth. Although the proposed land use category was residential, the specific uses have been identified as industrial in actuality, particularly in Barangay Maduya, and legal easement in Cabilang Baybay, wherein residential houses are present. The total affected population in Barangay Maduya is 10,551 or 2,942 households, while 7,120 individuals or 1,869 households are vulnerable to flooding hazards in Barangay Cabilang Baybay.

- b.4. Tourism Based on urban use disaster risk assessment, the 4.95 hectares of Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club (MSWGCC) at Barangay

Cabilang Baybay is exposed to moderate and high flood susceptibility with a very low vulnerability. The risk category for this tourism facility is moderate since this hazard occurs every 4-10 years. The expected flood depth for the moderate flooding susceptibility is less than or equal to 1m while high flooding susceptibility has greater than or equal to 1m flood depth.

- c. Critical Facilities Based on the critical facilities (school) disaster risk assessment, the Systems Technology Institute (STI)-Carmona Campus is susceptible to moderate

and high flooding. A moderate risk category is present in this institutional facility due to the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard, which is every 4 to 10 years. Moreover, a flood depth that is greater than or equal to 1 meter will affect the total land area of the private institutional facility, which is 7, 229.9 sq.m. The number of enrollees at risk to the flooding hazards is 362, composed of 204 males and 158 females, while the school personnel accounts for 44 teaching staff and nine (9) non-teaching staff.

![image 357](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile357.png>)

- d. Lifeline Utilities About 2,299.41 meters of roads in Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal have a moderate and high susceptibility to flooding. Barangay

Cabilang Baybay has the longest roads exposed to flooding with a moderate risk level, followed by Maduya (581.40 meters) and Bancal (59.04 meters). The expected flood depth for barangays with high flood susceptibility, including Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal, is greater than or equal to 1m. Meanwhile, moderate flood susceptibility (Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay) has a flood depth of less than or equal to 1m. Since the likelihood of occurrence is expected every 4 to years, the risk category is moderate.

Based on the assumption for the replacement cost of roads, including base failure, the average-cost would be P14, 560,888.50/km. Barangay Cabilang Baybay got the highest exposure percentage to flooding with 96.66%, while Barangay 6 is 100% exposed to landslides. In general, the municipality needs 33.4 million pesos if the affected roads with a total length of 2,299.41 meters are destroyed. The implementation of the existing regulations on hazard mitigation and structural design, along with the continuous monitoring of the compliance, helped in the attainment of the reduced magnitude of roads exposed to flooding and landslide.

- e. Natural Resource-Based Production Area Based on the climate and disaster risk assessment (natural resource-based production area), Barangays Maduya, Cabilang Baybay and Bancal are

susceptible to moderate and high flooding with moderate risk levels. The total agricultural land affected is 4.49 hectares and are currently utilized as rice fields, commercial and industrial establishments. Moderate flood susceptibility in Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay has less than or equal to 1m flood depth while high flood susceptibility has greater than or equal to 1m flood depth. Furthermore, since the likelihood of occurrence is moderate, those three barangays being mentioned will expect to experience this type of hazard every 4-10 years. Rivers in Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal are susceptible to moderate and high flooding hazards with moderate-risk level. The likelihood of occurrence for this type of vulnerability is every 4-10 years, affecting a total area of 16.16 hectares.

![image 361](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile361.png>)

- F.2. RISKS TO LANDSLIDE Based on the risk assessment, the Municipality is susceptible to rain-induced landslides affecting all barangays, especially in four of the systems of

interests (population, critical facilities, lifeline utilities, and urban use). Almost all of the exposed elements are low risks except for the exposure database on Natural Resource-Based Production Area (NRBPA) with moderate risks. The general descriptions of the landslide risks of different exposed elements are as follows:

- a. Population All barangays are at risk of landslide. Based on the population risk assessment, 82,706 people, or 86.75% of the total population are affected by the

low landslide, wherein 33,012 are young and old dependents, 682 are PWDs, and 120 are malnourished individuals. Meanwhile, about 15,246 families are affected, of which 1,738 are households living in light materials, 2752 are informal settlers, and 10,756 are households living below poverty thresholds, making them vulnerable to the impact of the landslide.

Furthermore, moderate landslides are possible at five barangays, including Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal, while high landslides are possible at Barangays Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal. About 1, 724 young and old dependents, 21 PWDs, 185 informal settlers, 105 households living in light materials, 1,158 households living below the poverty threshold, and 26 malnourished individuals will be affected by the moderate landslide. Meanwhile, high landslides will affect 146 informal settlers, 61 households living in light materials or salvageable materials, 2,064 young and old dependents, 37 PWDs, 923 households living below the poverty threshold, and 11 malnourished individuals.

Barangays with low susceptibility to landslide have low risk levels due to occasional (every 11 to 30 years) likelihood of occurrence, while barangays with moderate and high landslide susceptibility have improbable (every 31-100 years) likelihood of occurrence.

- b. Urban Use The municipality has an occasional likelihood of occurrence for landslide hazard, affecting all barangays at a low-risk level and very low

vulnerability. Based on the urban use risk assessment, most of the affected areas consist of residential, industrial, and commercial land uses. Facilities for Parks and Recreation have low landslide susceptibility, including the Municipal Plaza at Barangay 4, Municipal Park at Barangay Lantic, Villa Alegre

![image 365](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile365.png>)

Clubhouse, and Cityland Clubhouse at Barangay Mabuhay. Agricultural land areas in Barangays Maduya, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal are also susceptible to low landslide, affecting 26.97 hectares of land. Moreover, high and moderate landslides will affect 1.35 hectares of agricultural land in Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, and Lantic.

Moderate landslide is present at five barangays, affecting 121.43 hectares of commercial, industrial and residential land. Tourism facilities such as Firing Range in Barangay Milagrosa, the Manila Jockey Club in Barangay Lantic, and the Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club in Cabilang Baybay are also susceptible to moderate landslide with low-risk level. Furthermore, a total of 25.88 hectares of Ricefield in Barangay Maduya is susceptible to low landslide with low-risk level.

###### b.1. Parks and Recreation

The CDRA results for parks and recreation shows that five facilities are affected by exposure to landslide. These include one of the tourism facilities in the municipality, the Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club at Cabilang Baybay, two clubhouses (Villa Alegre and Cityland Clubhouse) in Barangay Mabuhay, Municipal Park at Barangay Lantic and the Town Plaza at Barangay 4. The likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional or is bound to experience every 11-30 years, making the risk level fall under the low category.

###### b.2. Agriculture Based on disaster risk assessment (urban use), the 25.88 hectares of Ricefield in Barangay Maduya is susceptible to low landslide with low-

risk level since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional (every 11 to 30 years).

###### b.3. Industrial Area A total area of 335.19 hectares of industrial lands from Barangay Rosario, Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal is

affected by a low landslide. The total affected population is 64,271 or 16,666 households, of which Barangay Lantic and Bancal got the highest number exposed to landslide hazards. On the other hand, industrial areas with moderate landslide susceptibility are identified at Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal, affecting 42.58 hectares. The total population exposed to moderate landslide is 50,694 or 12,866 households. Generally, the risk level is low since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional (every 4-10 years).

![image 369](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile369.png>)

High landslide susceptibility is also present in Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal. The total affected area is 51.53 hectares with a low-risk level and very low vulnerability. There is an occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard, which might be experienced every 11-30 years.

- b.4. Commercial Area A total of 73.65 hectares of commercial areas are exposed to landslides with low, moderate, and high susceptibility with 86,999 population

or 22,013 households affected. The risk level for these affected areas is relatively low due to the slight or occasional occurrence. Barangay Cabilang Baybay and Mabuhay are susceptible to moderate and high landslides affecting a total area of 15.07 hectares.

- b.5. Tourism The municipality has an occasional likelihood of occurrence for landslide hazard, affecting all barangays at a low-risk level and very low

vulnerability. Moderate landslide is present at five barangays, affecting 121.43 hectares of commercial, industrial and residential land. Tourism facilities such as Firing Range in Barangay Milagrosa, the Manila Jockey Club in Barangay Lantic, and the Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club in Cabilang Baybay are also susceptible to moderate landslide with low-risk level. The total affected area is 46.34 hectares, which has very low vulnerability to the impact of a landslide. Generally, the affected tourism facilities have low-risk levels to landslide hazards since the likelihood of occurrence is occasional (every 11 to 30 years).

- b.6. Housing The disaster risk assessment for urban use, two barangays (Maduya and Cabilang Baybay) are susceptible to moderate flooding that has a

frequent (every 1 to 3 years) likelihood of occurrence, and high flooding every 4 to 10 years, affecting a total residential area of 9.96 hectares. Barangays with moderate flooding susceptibility have less than or equal to 1m flood depth, while barangays with high flood susceptibility have greater than or equal to 1m flood depth. Although the proposed land use category was residential, the specific uses have been identified as industrial in actuality, particularly in Barangay Maduya, and legal easement in Cabilang Baybay, wherein residential houses are present. The total

![image 373](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile373.png>)

affected population in Barangay Maduya is 10,551 or 2,942 households, while 7,120 individuals or 1,869 households are vulnerable to flooding hazards in Barangay Cabilang Baybay.

- c. Critical Facilities Based on the critical facilities risk assessment, a total of 123 facilities are exposed to different categories of landslide susceptibility. All barangays

are exposed to landslide with low susceptibility and low risk level. Occasional landslide is possible in the municipality, affecting the Carmona Public Market, Municipal Trial Court, 14 barangay halls, 1 Evacuation Center, 1 Fire Station, 5 Government Offices, 13 Health Centers, 2 Hospitals, 1 Police Station, 12 Public Schools, 1 Cemetery, 10 Communication Towers, 12 Child Development Centers, 1 Jail Facility, 32 Fire Hydrants and 16 Pumping Stations.

Moderate landslide susceptibility was identified at Barangay Mabuhay affecting 164.6 sq. m. of pumping station, Child Development Centers (Paligawan and Davilan) and Paligawan Health Center at Barangay Lantic and hydrant at Barangay Milagrosa. Meanwhile, high landslide susceptibility is possible at Paligawan Elementary School in Barangay Lantic, Milagrosa Phase 4 Child Development Center, Health Center and Pumping Station in Barangay Mabuhay. Furthermore, the CARSIGMA Evacuation Center is also vulnerable to low risk level of landslide, affecting its total land area of 4,232.2 sq. m.

Furthermore, ten (10) Communication towers at various barangays are also vulnerable to landslide hazard, affecting 6,421.2 sq. m. of land. These towers are composed of Globe and Smart Telecom at Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay and Bancal, with low landslide susceptibility and low risk level.

- c.1. Education All Public institutions at elementary, secondary, and tertiary levels are susceptible to landslides with very low vulnerability and low-risk

levels, except Paligawan Elementary School at Barangay Lantic that has high susceptibility to the landslide. The total number of affected enrollees from 9 public elementary schools, two (2) public secondary schools (junior and senior high), and a public tertiary school is 19,082. Of this number, 9,469 are male, and 9,613 are female. The highest magnitude of enrollees affected is from Carmona National High School, situated in Barangay Rosario, followed by the only public tertiary school in the municipality (CavSU) with 2,313 enrollees. Moreover, the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional (every 11 to 30 years), resulting in the low-risk category.

![image 377](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile377.png>)

- c.2. Health Low landslide susceptibility with the low-risk level was assessed at various health centers in the municipality, excluding the Paligawan

Health Center in Barangay Lantic and Phase 4 Health Center in Barangay Milagrosa, with moderate to high landslide susceptibility, respectively. The affected barangays have a low-risk level since the likelihood of occurrence for the landslide hazard is occasional (every 11 to 30 years). Moreover, two (2) hospital facilities situated in Barangay 4 and 11 (Pagamutang Bayan ng Carmona (PBC) and the Carmona Hospital and Medical Center) covering a total area of 5,101.1 sq. m. are affected by the landslide.

A total of 482 personnel from public health facilities are susceptible to hazards brought by a landslide, with Milagrosa Health Center having the highest magnitude of personnel (100), followed by the Maduya Health Center and Bancal Health Center, with 68 and 44 personnel, respectively.

- c.3. Housing With the occasional landslide that is possible to hit the municipality, the residential areas from all barangays covering a total area of 305.65

hectares are susceptible to landslides with low-risk levels. Furthermore, based on the approved land use category, the following are residential areas in the municipality; however, the specific uses are commercial, industrial, agricultural, legal easement, and Local Water District, where some families reside. The total affected population is 95,338 or 24,338 households. The highest magnitude is situated in barangays Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal.

Furthermore, residential areas in Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal are susceptible to moderate and high landslides. About 74,511 individuals or 18,479 households will be affected by moderate and high landslides. A total area of 67.28 hectares will be affected by moderate landslides, while a high landslide will affect 45.30 hectares of residential areas. The affected barangays have a very low vulnerability and low-risk levels for this type of hazard. However, a total of 0.81 hectares of residential areas in Barangays Mabuhay and Bancal are susceptible to a very high landslide, affecting a total population of 23,877 or 6,524 households. These barangays have occasional landslide occurrence as well that made the risk category fall under the low level.

![image 381](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile381.png>)

- c.4. Social Welfare All Child Development Centers in the municipality are exposed to landslide with low susceptibility and low-risk level, except Milagrosa

Phase 4 Child Development Center in Barangay Milagrosa with high susceptibility, and moderate landslide susceptibility at Paligawan and Davilan Child Development Center in Barangay Lantic. These critical facilities have a very low vulnerability to the impact of the landslide, affecting a total area of 1,052.1 sq. m. Furthermore, the CARSIGMA Evacuation Center is also susceptible to a low-risk level of a landslide, affecting its total land area of 4,232.2 sq. m. The occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard made these critical facilities fall under a low-risk category.

About 1,116 pupils from various Child Development Centers are exposed to landslide hazards at a low-risk category since there is an occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard. Child Development Center in Barangay Bancal got the highest magnitude of pupils affected with 158, followed by Cabilang Baybay (132), Lantic (125), Milagrosa Phase 3 (105), and Mabuhay with 100 pupils.

- c.5. Protective Services Three of the major protective services facilities, namely, the Bureau of Fire Protection (BFP), Bureau of Jail Management and Penology

(BJMP) and the Carmona Municipal Police Station at Barangay Maduya with a total land area of 894.3 sq. m. are exposed to landslide with low susceptibility and low risk level. Due to occasional likelihood of landslide occurrence, these three (3) protective services facilities have low risk category, affecting 121 personnel. Of the affected population, 22 are BFP personnel, 16 are BJMP personnel and 83 are policemen.

- c.6. Institutional Facilities All barangay halls are exposed to landslide with very low vulnerability, low susceptibility, and risk level. The Bureau of Fire Protection,

Philippine National Police, Post Office, and Bureau of Jail Management and Penology (BJMP)-Carmona located inside the DILG-Compound in Barangay Maduya are also susceptible to low landslide affecting 1,065.5 sq. m. Other Government-owned buildings susceptible to landslide are the Municipal Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Office (MDRRMO), Carmona Municipal Hall, BML Building I and II, with a total land area of 1,794,4 sq. m. affected. The Carmona Public Market (CPM) and Municipal Trial Court are also exposed to landslides with low susceptibility and lowrisk levels.

![image 385](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile385.png>)

Due to occasional or slight chance of landslide occurrence, the following critical facilities have low-risk levels. This hazard will affect 2,037 individuals, of which 1,334 are barangay personnel while 703 are municipal personnel composed of 468 males and 235 females.

- c.7. Communication Facilities and Water Facilities Ten (10) Communication towers at various barangays are also vulnerable to landslide hazards, affecting 6,421.2 sq. m. of land. These

towers are composed of Globe and Smart Telecom at Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, and Bancal, with low landslide susceptibility and low-risk level. The affected barangays have low-risk categories since the landslide occurrence is occasional (every 11 to 30 years).

In terms of water facilities, a total of thirty-two (32) hydrants situated at Barangays 4, 5, 8, 9, 10, 11, and 12 are exposed to landslides with low susceptibility, while Barangay Milagrosa has moderate landslide susceptibility. With the occasional likelihood of occurrence, the affected barangays have a low-risk level to this type of hazard.

Sixteen (16) pumping stations with a total area of 4,407 sq. m. are affected by possible landslides in the municipality. These are located at Barangays Lantic, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Cabilang Baybay, Maduya, and Bancal. Fourteen of which have low susceptibility, while the remaining two (2) pumping stations situated at Barangay Mabuhay have moderate and high landslide susceptibility. Due to the occasional (every 11-30 years) occurrence of landslide hazards, the affected barangays have a low-risk category.

- c.8. Cemetery The Sanctuario de Carmona, which covers a total land area of 62,278.6 sq. m. in Barangay Maduya, is also exposed to landslide, with low

susceptibility, vulnerability, and risk level. Due to occasional or slight chance of landslide occurrence, the cemetery has a low-risk category.

![image 389](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile389.png>)

- d. Lifeline Utilities About 192,761.12 meters of roads have a low, moderate, and high susceptibility to the landslide. Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa,

Lantic, and Bancal are susceptible to moderate and high landslides, while all barangays are susceptible to a low landslide. The longest length of roads exposed to landslides with a low-risk level is located in Barangay Lantic, with 56,885.89 meters. The likelihood of occurrence is very rare (every <200 years, which made the identified barangays at a low-risk level to landslide hazards.

Meanwhile, water pipelines exposed at landslide with low-risk levels are expected at all barangays in Carmona, with a total length of 45,805.32 meters affected. Low landslide susceptibility is expected in all barangays, affecting a total of 43,486.50 meters of water pipelines. Furthermore, about 876.15 meters of water pipelines in three barangays (Mabuhay, Milagrosa, and Bancal) are susceptible to moderate landslide, and high landslide susceptibility will affect a total of 1,442.73 meters of water pipelines in Barangays Mabuhay and Milagrosa. Generally, these barangays have low-risk levels since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is very rare (every >200 years).

The longest water pipelines affected by a landslide are Barangay Mabuhay and Milagrosa, consisting of 11,214.99 meters and 10,098.07 meters, respectively. Barangay Maduya followed with a total length of 8,906.21 meters of pipelines affected. It is assumed that in a worst-case scenario, the estimated replacement cost for affected water pipelines (flooding) is PhP 179,935.00, and PhP 160,318,620.00 is the expected replacement cost to landslide hazards.

- e. Natural Resource-Based Production Area

- e.1. Agricultural Lands At Risk to Landslide Agricultural land areas at Barangays 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, and 14 are susceptible to low landslides with low-risk levels. A total area of 494.99

hectares with the specific use of commercial, industrial, residential, and the remaining portion solely for agricultural activities are affected by the landslide at a low-risk level.

The disaster risk assessment (NRBPA) also shows Barangays Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal have moderate landslide susceptibility. The total agricultural lands affected are 278.95 hectares with low-risk levels due to its improbable likelihood of occurrence (every 31-100 years).

![image 393](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile393.png>)

High landslide susceptibility is also present with moderate risk category at Barangays 10, 11, 13, and 14, and low-risk category at Barangays 11 to 14. The total affected area for barangays susceptible to high landslide with moderate risk levels is 654.81 hectares, while 9.72 hectares for the low-risk category. Furthermore, Barangay Bancal has very high exposure to landslides with a low-risk level. Since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is improbable, the risk category level is low and moderate.

- e.2. Rivers and Creeks At Risk to Landslide About 52.88 hectares of rivers and creeks from ten (10) Barangays are affected by occasional landslides in the municipality. The

susceptibility and vulnerability of these barangays to landslides are low. In particular, rivers situated in Barangay San Pablo, Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic and Bancal have low susceptibility to landslide along with irrigation canals at Barangay Rosario and Maduya, and creeks at various barangays (6, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12 and 13). The affected barangays have a low-risk level due to the occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard.

Four barangays (Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal) have moderate susceptibility to landslide, affecting a total area of 20.44 hectares of rivers and creeks. These barangays are also susceptible to high landslides, with a total area of 1.47 hectares affected, and 2.51 hectares in Mabuhay and Bancal will be affected by a very high landslide. The risk category for these barangays is generally low since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional (every 11 to 30 years).

![image 397](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile397.png>)

#### G. HYDROLOGY AND WATER RESOURCES

- G.1. WATER RESOURCES Carmona falls within the Biñan River Basin as well as the Laguna de Bay Region Administrative Jurisdiction. It covers an area of about 81 sq. kms

with an area of about 81 sq. kms, with an annual rainfall depth over the basin estimated at 2, 637 millimeters and an annual surface run-off depth of 1, 166 mm. Surface water resources of Carmona is sourced from rivers such as the Carmona River, Soro-soro, Calabuso, Cahilan, Manawa, Embarkadero, Asuntoy and Pasong Pare as well as from springs in particular Bucal and the Munting Ilog Spring. The river systems of Carmona are connected to the rivers of Silang and Dasmariñas and empty towards Laguna de Bay. Carmona rivers and its tributaries are being used as source of water for irrigation purposes. Munting Ilog Spring in Silang flows down to Munting Ilog in Carmona. Munting Ilog Spring and Carmona River irrigate the agricultural areas of Lantic, Ligdang and Maduya. The Lantic dam located in Barangay Lantic which is composed of Prinsang Luma and Prinsang Bago derive its water supply from the Carmona River.

The groundwater of Carmona has an average well depth of 116 meters, average static level of 52 meters below ground surface and average specific capacity of 0.24 liters per second per meter of drawdown (NWRDB Rapid Assessment of Water Supply for Cavite, 1982). As part of the larger Biñan River Basin, the groundwater of Carmona is characterized as having a recharge of about 8 mcm per year and an annual discharge of 39 mcm. Groundwater pumpage for the whole basin is computed at 10 mcm per year.

Today, some areas like Cabilang Baybay and other parts of Poblacion have been depending on the Carmona Water District for water supply as potable water cannot be drawn from the average 52 meters below the ground surface. Instead, submersible pumps are the only way to draw water at 300 meters. It should be noted that based on the same study, the groundwater of Carmona decreases almost a meter every year and is expected to reach its limit by 2015. Hence, it is crucial to determine possible alternative water resources that can be utilized by the people in the future.

One of the strategies that the LGU will undertake to provide the residents of Carmona with alternative water resources is through the conduct of the Carmona River Profiling, which might be used by the LGU for future planning activities. This initiative will help the LGU establish its updated natural water systems inventory and baseline information that will later be of great importance in transforming the 30% of the municipality’s natural water systems to Class C for domestic, commercial, recreational, and industrial by 2022.

![image 401](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile401.png>)

###### B.3. URBANIZATION LEVEL

In Carmona, the urbanization levels for the past twenty (20) years showed a significant increase not only with its population moving into the poblacion areas but to the economic performance of the poblacion areas. Carmona was recognized as the first-class municipality in the province of Cavite, through the economic development driven by numerous industrial and commercial establishments. A significant number of residential subdivisions have been completed with a few more in the development. These have driven and continue to drive the dynamism of the municipality.

![image 442](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile442.png>)

However, urbanization also presents many human development challenges, such as environmental concerns or the vulnerability to natural disasters like storms and floods. Poor urban infrastructure such as congested roads, poor public transport, unreliable power systems, and inadequate schools also reduces municipalities' competitiveness and economic prospects. Hence, not all urbanization is positive, especially if it is unplanned.

###### B.5. HIERARCHY OF SETTLEMENTS BY BARANGAY The hierarchy of settlements was categorized according to the

population density, population per barangay, and urbanization level of the locality. Based on the 2017-2017 CBMS Survey Results, Barangay Milagrosa has the highest population with 23,817, followed by Barangay Lantic with 19,697 and Barangay Bancal with 14,244. In terms of urbanization level of each barangay, most of the residential subdivisions are located at Barangay 13 Lantic while the manufacturing industries are mostly from Barangay Bancal.

Source: CBMS 2017-2018

Furthermore, the highest built-up density recorded is 593.50

persons per hectare in Barangay Milagrosa, followed by 510.39 persons per hectare in Barangay Cabilang Baybay and 498.52 persons per hectare in Barangay 8. Barangays Lantic and Bancal became the growth centers of the municipality due to its location being suitable for business establishments.

#### C. POPULATION CHANGES

###### C.1. HISTORICAL GROWTH OF POPULATION

Carmona had the high urbanization over its existence, in the years 1970, 2008, and 2011, it had 6.98% and 6.64%, population growth respectively. Since 1970s, numerous commercial, industrial and residential subdivisions were developed to absorb the population growth and drive urbanization.

The decrease in growth rate in 1980 at 2.68% was due to the creation of these two barrios into the Municipality of General Mariano Alvarez (GMA) under Proclamation No. 2033. Carmona’s population is continuously increasing with a growth rate pattern not higher than 7% every year but significantly higher than the national rate. As of 2017, the total population of Carmona is 95,338, with an average population growth rate of 2.67%.

- C.2. FERTILITY AND MORTALITY The crude birth rate was significantly reduced to 7.92% in 2019. This is the lowest rate recorded for the past five years. The crude death

rate increased by 0.09%, from 3.08% in 2018 to 3.17% in 2019. The leading cause of Mortality this year is Acute Myocardial Infarction with 87 cases, while the leading cause of Morbidity is Acute Upper Respiratory Tract Infection with 11,913 cases.

![image 463](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile463.png>)

Rapid urbanization and unhealthy lifestyle have led to the prevalence of Non-Communicable Diseases (NCDs) in the municipality. These include diabetes, hypertension, heart disease, obesity, cancer, stroke, chronic lung, and kidney diseases that contributed to the mortality rate in the municipality. Similarly, increased cases of alcohol and smoke-related illnesses and deaths will continue unless there will be a healthy lifestyle by all concerned citizens in the community.

###### D.2. MOTHER TONGUE/ ETHNICITY

The Philippines has eight (8) major dialects, namely, Bikol, Cebuano, Hiligaynon (Ilonggo), Ilocano, Kapampangan, Pangasinan (Pangalatok), Tagalog and Waray. These languages are among the 115 ethnic dialects that exist in the Municipality of Carmona, Cavite. The following ethnicities have their district dialects or language by members of a particular ethnic group. Out of the total population of 95,338, Tagalog is the most used dialect in Carmona, comprised of 27,945 individuals, while in ethnicity; Caviteños got the highest number with 35,642 individuals.

- D.4. LITERACY RATE In 2017, the municipality's literacy rate was 97.85%. Out of 74,775 population of age ten years old and above, there were 73,169 literates and 1,606

or 2.15% illiterates. Barangay Milagrosa has the highest magnitude of literate persons with 18, 101 in total, followed by Lantic with 14, 409 literate persons, and Barangay Bancal with 10, 731.

The literacy rate can be classified as Functional Literacy, Simple Literacy, and High Literacy. Functional literacy is defined as the ability to read, write, and perform simple mathematical calculations. Simple or Basic Literacy, on the other hand, is the ability of persons age 10 years old and above to read, write and understand simple messages in any language or dialect. Lastly, High literacy is the ability of using the written word (and making simple arithmetic calculations) in daily life, and to continue learning.

Currently, there are 1,606 illiterate persons in the municipality or 2.15% of the population. It is above the 91.6% national literacy rate. Lack of information collected on the reason that illiteracy is a gap in the current data points. Nonetheless, the municipality will be investing in increasing the literacy of the population of Carmona to continue driving the economic growth of the municipality.

- D.5. FILIPINOS OVERSEAS As per the 2017 CBMS Survey, there are 24,338 households in Carmona with 92,397 resident populations and 2,940 Filipino overseas. Out of this

Filipino Overseas, 2,860 are Overseas Filipino Workers (OFWs), and 80 are non-working overseas individuals. Most of these Filipinos Overseas are male.

Overseas Filipinos Workers play a significant role towards the economic development of the municipality with remittances both of OFWs from Carmona but nearby municipalities and cities providing a major source of economic development.

The table above shows the projected population and households of Carmona in 10 years. With the current population of 95,338, the comprehensive approach of the municipality is necessary to cater to the rapidly growing demands for public services and infrastructure of the people. Moreover, an increase in the household population will create income and opportunity disparities, affecting the sustainability of development in the municipality.

Being one with the United Nations Development Programme's (UNDP) goals to alleviate poverty in all its forms and dimensions, a local government like Carmona must strengthen its intergovernmental relationships. This is to ensure that urbanization will not compromise the adequacy of sanitation, safe

![image 504](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile504.png>)

drinking water, and other environmental concerns, which will risk the lives of the people, particularly among children. Thus, national, regional, and municipality/city governments need to be connected and share a similar vision to achieve successful urbanization and sustainable development.

###### E.2. POPULATION PROJECTION BY BARANGAY

Population projection is a fundamental requirement in planning, particularly in the investments for school buildings, teacher training, and the production of educational materials. Moreover, this serves as a basis for estimating the future needs for health services, determining the level of future demand for food, power, water, transport, facilities, and utilities, as well as the corresponding spatial requirements. By the end of the planning period in 2030, the estimated population of Carmona will be 130,794.

- F. POPULATION EXPOSURE AND/OR RISK ASSESSMENT TO HAZARDS The Municipality of Carmona, Cavite has a total population of 95,338 and a household population of 24,338. Of this number, 55,844 are exposed to

both flooding and landslide hazards. These are composed of 3,086 informal settlers from Barangays 9 through 14, Bancal being the highest magnitude with 1,897; followed by Cabilang Baybay (432), Lantic (286), and Maduya (241). About 1,922 households are living in light materials, 12,930 are households living below the poverty threshold, and 157 are malnourished individuals. Moreover, vulnerable groups such as young and old dependents, as well as Persons with Disabilities (PWD's) account for 37,001 and 748 individuals, respectively.

Most of the young and old dependents are situated in Barangay Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal, while Barangay Milagrosa, Lantic and Maduya have the most number of PWDs.

###### F.1.POPULATION AT RISK TO FLOODING

Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay are at risk of flooding with moderate and high susceptibility. Expected flood depth for moderate susceptibility is less than or equal to 1m, and greater than or equal to 1m for high flooding susceptibility. Fifty-seven (57) households or 216 family members are affected by moderate flooding, while thirty-one (31) households or 139 family members are affected by high flooding with moderate risk level at Barangay Maduya. This area is frequently affected by flooding events since this barangay is situated near the Carmona River.

Meanwhile, the affected population in Barangay Cabilang Baybay is composed of 118 family members or 31 households susceptible to moderate flooding, and 114 family members or 27 households susceptible to high flooding. Moreover, CDRA Results show that a total of 355 people or 88 households are projected to be affected in varying degrees depending on their identified sensitivities to flooding. Of these numbers, 161 are young and old dependents, 8 are Persons with Disabilities (PWDs), and 54 are exposed households living below the poverty threshold.

It is noteworthy that there is a diminished risk level to flooding hazards due to the high level of awareness and adaptive capacities of its residents and the extensive disaster risk reduction measures and programs of the Municipal Government. The affected barangays with moderate flooding susceptibility have moderate risk levels since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is bound to experience every 1 to 3 years, while the high flooding susceptibility occurs every 4 to 10 years.

###### F.2.POPULATION AT RISK TO LANDSLIDE

All barangays are at risk of some sort of landslide. Based on the population risk assessment, 82,706 people, or 86.75% of the total population are affected by a landslide hazard, wherein 33,012 are young and old dependents, 682 are PWDs, and 120 are malnourished individuals. About 15,246 families are affected by the hazard, of which 1,738 are households living in light materials, 2,752 are informal settlers, and 10,756 are households living below poverty thresholds, making them vulnerable to the impact of a landslide.

Furthermore, moderate landslides are possible at five barangays, including Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal, while high landslides are possible at Barangays Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal. About 1, 724 young and old dependents, 21 PWDs, 185 informal settlers, 105 households living in light materials, 1,158 households living below the poverty threshold, and 26 malnourished individuals will be affected by the moderate landslide. Meanwhile, high landslides will affect 146 informal settlers, 61 households living in light materials or salvageable materials, 2,064 young and old dependents, 37 PWDs, 923 households living below the poverty threshold, and 11 malnourished individuals.

Barangays with low susceptibility to landslide have low risk levels due to occasional (every 11 to 30 years) likelihood of occurrence, while barangays with moderate and high landslide susceptibility have improbable (every 31-100 years) likelihood of occurrence.

### Chapter 4: SOCIAL SECTOR

#### A. EDUCATION

###### A.1. ELEMENTARY AND SECONDARY

Education services are offered through public and private institutions. As of 2019, Carmona has 23 pre-elementary schools (9 public, 14 private), 21 elementary schools (9 public, 12 private), 9 junior high schools (1 public, 8 private) and 4 senior high schools (1 public, 3 private). Moreover, the municipality has a total of two tertiary schools, and a private/technical/vocational school.

Facilities of these schools were inspected to see which institutions need prioritization in educational infrastructure development and maintenance. Looking at the data below, only Milagrosa Elementary School has no available canteen for the enrollees. Clinics are not yet available at Bancal, Lantic, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa Elementary School (West), and Paligawan Matanda Elementary School; and all facilities at Carmona National High School (CNHS) are in dire need of improvement and priority action. Also, the establishment of libraries for all schools in the municipality are in need of some sort of additional investment. Other facilities that need priority action are the principal’s guidance office and canteen at CNHS and Bancal Elementary School and comfort rooms at Paligawan Matanda Elementary School and CNHS.

The table above shows the current student-teacher ratio and the student-classroom ratio of both public and private schools offering elementary and secondary (junior and senior) levels of education. The total number of teachers in public and private elementary schools is 306 and 84, respectively. These teachers conduct classes in approximately 209 public school classrooms and 81 private school classrooms.

Due to the increasing number of school-age population at Barangay Milagrosa, Lantic and Bancal, public elementary schools and secondary (junior high) school require additional classrooms to meet the standard ratio of 1:40. Furthermore, as these barangays are set to keep growing, significant resources should be poured in order to maintain the high literacy and quality of graduates of Carmona. Private senior-high schools also reflected a need for additional classrooms and teachers, particularly at STI-College Southwoods, with only three (3) available classrooms; and four (4) teachers supervising 332 students.

![image 554](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile554.png>)

- A.2. TERTIARY AND VOCATIONAL/TECHNICAL The Municipality has two (2) tertiary schools. Cavite State University, the first and only public tertiary school in Carmona, has a total of

2,313 enrollees for SY 2019-2020. CAVSU has 69 full-time Teachers and 22 Classrooms. The System Technology Institute (STI), on the other hand, has a total of 332 enrollees with 44-full time Teachers and 25 available classrooms. Imus Computer College (ICC) is the only vocational private school in Carmona with 244 enrollees for SY 2019-2020.

###### A.3. HISTORICAL ENROLLMENT BY LEVEL

Data shows the population and the percentage rate of increase/decrease of enrollment for each level in both public and private schools for the last three (3) years. It is noticeable that School Year 2017-2018 had the lowest figures for the past three years, with a total of 19,363 students. Currently, a total of 22, 195 students were enrolled at public and private institutions of the municipality. This is relatively higher by 2.07% compared to the previously reported number of enrollees. Moreover, increasing number of secondary enrollees was observed from both public and private schools this year, while enrollees from elementary and tertiary level showed an increasing and decreasing thread of numbers.

Carmona National High School (CNHS) is the public secondary school in Carmona that serves the junior high-school students. With the increasing number of enrollees at CNHS, the need of its students for additional classrooms must be taken into consideration. This will deter the provision of quality education services due to the overcrowded classrooms. Thus, the establishment of Benjamin Loyola Science High School in Barangay Lantic must be instigated to provide these students with quality education.

Public high school requires more classrooms than public elementary schools due to the continuous growth in the volume of secondary school students. In addition, the rapid growth or increase in the number of enrollees for public high schools necessitates for additional teachers, as well as access to sanitary toilet facilities to ensure quality education among all students. For the stand-alone Senior High School Building at Barangay Lantic, the provision of adequate number of teachers by 2023 is crucial to ensure effective implementation of the K-12 program.

In terms of tertiary education, Cavite State University (CvSU) – Carmona, which is the only public tertiary school of Carmona, Cavite has seen an increase in the number of its enrollees in last three (3) years. The institution has 69 full-Time teachers and 22 classrooms. With the existing classroom-student ratio of 1:60, CvSU-Carmona falls short in the standard ratio for tertiary school which is 1:40; hence, additional 24 classrooms are needed. Meanwhile, with the existing teacher-student ratio of 1: 32, there is no need for additional teachers until 2022.

A 4-storey-20-classroom will be constructed in Cabilang Baybay Elementary School by 2022, Lantic Elementary School for three years (20242026), Paligawan Matanda Elementary School by 2025, and Bancal Elementary School by 2023 and 2025. Meanwhile, a 3-storey-6-classroom will be constructed in Cabilang Baybay Elementary School by 2023, and Mabuhay Elementary School by 2025. Maduya Elementary School and Paligawan Matanda Elementary School will require a 4-storey-16-classroom by 2023 and 2024, respectively. Nevertheless, a 3-storey Multi-Purpose Building will be constructed at Angelo Levardo Loyola Senior High School by 2026 to augment needed classrooms for the succeeding years.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (EDUCATION SUB-SECTOR)

![image 574](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile574.png>)

###### A. SCHOOLS AT RISK TO FLOODING Based on the critical facilities (school)

disaster risk assessment, the Systems Technology Institute (STI)-Carmona Campus is susceptible to moderate and high flooding. A moderate risk category is present in this institutional facility due to the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard, which is every 4 to 10 years. Moreover, a flood depth that is greater than or equal to 1 meter will affect the total land area of the private institutional facility, which is 7, 229.9 sq.m. The number of enrollees at risk to the flooding hazards is 362, composed of 204 males and 158 females, while the school personnel accounts for 44 teaching staff and nine (9) nonteaching staff.

- B. SCHOOLS AT RISK TO LANDSLIDE All Public institutions at elementary, secondary, and tertiary levels are susceptible to some fort of landslides with very low vulnerability and low-risk

levels, except Paligawan Elementary School at Barangay Lantic that has high susceptibility to the landslide. The total number of affected enrollees from 9 public elementary schools, two (2) public secondary schools (junior and senior high), and a public tertiary school is 19,082. Of this number, 9,469 are male, and 9,613 are female. The highest magnitude of enrollees affected is from Carmona National High School, situated in Barangay Rosario, followed by the only public tertiary school in the municipality (CavSU) with 2,313 enrollees. Moreover, the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional (every 11 to 30 years), resulting in the low-risk category.

|Insufficient relevant academic research pursuits|Faculty members are still not into research (faculty members lack confidence to pursue researches); lack of training in research; faculty are loaded with instruction related functions|Unable to comply to any accreditation institution| Research proposals<br> Research grants<br> Research publications<br> Research outputs to be disseminated and extended to the community<br> Partner research agencies<br>|
|Low retention rate|Student factor (lack of financial support for the students)|Low enrolment rate would mean lesser budget for campus expenditure| Financial assistance programs<br> Scout for additional scholarship donors<br>|
|Absence of a safety warning device along the Market Drive main road (Campus frontage)|Insufficient budget|Students and employees are prone to road accidents|Installation of safety warning device (movable purlin)|

#### B. HEALTH AND SANITATION

###### B.1. GENERAL HEALTH SITUATION

The overwhelming drive towards better health services resulted in a healthy citizenry as shown on the health indices below:

###### Table 4-9. General Health Situation for the Last Five Years

|Health Indicator|2015|2016|2017|2018|2019|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|Fertility| | | | | |
|Crude Birth Rates (CBR)|15.91|8.4|9.19|8.73|0.81|
|Total Fertility Rate (TFR)| | | | | |
|Mortality| | | | | |
|Crude Death Rate (CDR)|3.3|3.7|3.66|3.08|3.17|
|Proportioned Mortality Rate (PMR)| | | | | |
|Infant Mortality Rate (IMR)|4.1|1.11|1.12|0.7|11.11|
|Young Mortality Rate (YMR)|0.04|1.6|1.56|0.9|12.22|
|Maternal Mortality Rate (MMR)|0|0|0|0|0|

Source: MHO

A significant decrease in the crude birth rate was first recorded in 2016, due to the observance of family planning program among households. In 2019, the crude birth rate recorded its lowest percentage with 0.81%, while the crude death rate increased to 3.17% compared to its previous record of 3.08%. Infant and young mortality rate also increased with 11.11% and 12.22%, respectively.

![image 626](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile626.png>)

The Municipality of Carmona owns and operates a public hospital, the Pagamutang Bayan ng Carmona (PBC) with 15-bed-capacity, 1 Main Health Center, and 13 Barangay Health Stations (BHS). All health facilities are equipped with modern medical/laboratory equipment and medicines/supplies. At present, the newly-built Pagamutang Bayan ng Carmona (PBC) has the Emergency Room, Delivery Room, and Ancillary Services section to comply with the standards of the Department of Health (DOH) for hospital accreditation.

The Municipal Health Office and Pagamutang Bayan ng Carmona (PBC) is headed by the Municipal Health Officer with 23 doctors, 23 nurses, 17 midwives, 2 sanitary inspectors, 1 physical therapist, 4 medical technologists, 2 dentists, 91 Barangay Health Workers, and 320 Family Health Workers. The total medical personnel for 2019 are 483.

As the population increases, the need for additional health workers also increases. Health personnel to population ratio still require two (2) doctors, three (3) nurses and adequate number of midwives for the succeeding years. Dentists and nurses are enough to cover the total population.

Over the past years, acute upper respiratory infection is the leading cause of morbidity in the municipality. In 2019, 10.73% or 11, 913 cases were reported to the Municipal Health Office. This is 5.4% higher than the previous year’s record of 11, 303 reported incidents. The morbidity or the condition of being ill, diseased or unhealthy of the people points out the following enumerated infections with the urinary tract infection as the second cause of morbidity. Dengue and aging of the people have entered the list comprising 1.02% and 0.57% of the total number of deaths, respectively.

Acute Myocardial Infarction, which is the medical name for Heart Attack, is the leading cause of Mortality in the country. It is also the primary cause of death by some people in the municipality of Carmona. Based on the data shown above, reported deaths increased by 27.94%, from 68 deaths in 2018 to 87 deaths in 2019. Pneumonia and Electrolyte Imbalance followed with 50 and 40 reported incidents, respectively.

These are children classified as Normal: 7,368 (87%); Overweight: 457 (6%); Obese: 354 (4%); Wasted: 192 (2%); and Severely Wasted: 82 (1%). The year 2017 has the highest reported cases of malnourished children with 10, 794, while the following years (2018 and 2019) remained the same with 8,453 cases.
