- B.7. INVENTORY OF CEMETERIES AND MEMORIAL PARKS

The Sanctuario de Carmona (SDC) is the only cemetery in the municipality which is located in Barangay Maduya. This was established in May 2006 with an area of 5.0336 hectares. In 2015, the old municipal cemetery in Cabilang Baybay was converted into a municipal park. SDC has a bigger capacity than the old cemetery. Most of those interred in the old cemetery were transferred to the new facility. SDC is a well-planned cemetery modeled from privately maintained cemeteries where the cost of interment is kept at a minimum. All types of burial tomb from mausoleum to the apartment-type are available depending on financial capability.

###### B.8. HOUSEHOLD DISTRIBUTION BY TYPE OF TOILET FACILITY

A total of 20, 086, or 82.58% of the total households have a water-sealed flush to sewer/septic tank systems while some are sharing the same system amongst them. The rest of the household populace either uses the closed-pit or open-pit system while a small number have no toilet system at all or are using other means of sewerage disposal.

###### B.9. SOLID WASTE AND WASTEWATER FACILITIES

Management of solid wastes has been among the primary mandate of the local government in terms of the environment. Thru the Municipal Environment & Natural Resources (MENRO), various Solid Waste Management Programs are being carried out: Basura Palit Gamit Atbp., Ecobag ni Mayor Loyola, Carmona Eco-livelihood and AKO BASURERO on top of the regular waste collection of recyclables, biodegradables, and residuals among others.

In 2019, the total waste generated among residential and small commercial was 11, 410.28 metric tons (mt). These are composed of 2,743,73 mt (24%) recyclable wastes, including papers, plastics, bottles/glass, and metals; 3,543.98 mt (31%) biodegradable wastes; 114.32 mt (1%) special wastes and 5,008.25 (44%) are residual wastes.

###### a. Solid Waste Management Program

The municipality has initiated various programs promoting solid waste management that starts within the community. These programs include Basura Palit Gamit Atbp., Ecobag ni Mayor Loyola, Puno ng Bayan Tree Planting Program, and International Coastal Clean-up Month. Ecobag ni Mayor Loyola is part of the continued implementation of Municipal Ordinance No. 004-09, otherwise known as the selective Plastic Ban and Ecobag Ordinance. This program conducts monitoring and inspections of commercial establishments to ensure that the community complies with the said ordinance.

|Commercial Waste Collected through private hauling as per MO-006-07<br><br>|
|Industrial Waste Collected through private hauling as per MO-006-07|
|Hospital Waste Collected through private hauling as per MO-006-07<br><br>|
|Others Waste Collected through private hauling as per MO-006-07|

Source: MENRO

The table above shows the generated solid waste and the method or system of collection and disposal for the year 2019. Mostly, the collection system is done thru Barangay and Municipal garbage trucks that collect either twice or once a week. As classified, residual wastes are being collected twice a week by MENRO and barangay trucks; biodegradable wastes are daily collected from households and Carmona Public Market (CPM), while recyclable wastes are collected and being stored at Barangay Material Recovery System (MRS) and then transferred to Central MRF which is commonly known as the Carmona Ecology Center through the Basura Palit Gamit Atbp Program.

Disposal system/method was also listed above by which the residual wastes are disposed at Pilotage Trading and Construction through landfilling; biodegradables, on the other hand are disposed by the means of composting at the Carmona Ecology Center and lastly, recyclables are disposed through sorting and later, it will be sold to private recycling facilities that will process it.

![image 683](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile683.png>)

In terms of toxic and hazardous wastes, the municipality has no existing Hazard Waste Facility - TSD (Treatment, Storage, or Disposal), to monitor and control the use of toxic substances and disposal of hazardous wastes, and other industrial wastes. Exposure to these toxic wastes may bring severe effects to humans, causing death or violent illness, or chronic effects, slowly causing irreparable harm.

Under the Republic Act 9003, every local government units are responsible for the management of special wastes. However, this has been the most costly among the various types of solid wastes due to the intricacy, severity, and variety of handling, collection, transfer, and disposal involved. The absence of a special waste management system in the municipality implicates non-compliance with RA 9003 and poses environmental damage and health risks for people due to mishandling and non-management of special or hazardous wastes.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (HEALTH AND SANITATION FACILITIES)

- A. HEALTH FACILITIES AT RISK TO LANDSLIDE Low landslide susceptibility with the low-risk level was assessed at various health centers in the municipality, excluding the Paligawan Health Center

in Barangay Lantic and Phase 4 Health Center in Barangay Milagrosa, with moderate to high landslide susceptibility, respectively. The affected barangays have a low-risk level since the likelihood of occurrence for the landslide hazard is occasional (every 11 to 30 years). Moreover, two (2) hospital facilities situated in Barangay 4 and 11 (Pagamutang Bayan ng Carmona (PBC) and the Carmona Hospital and Medical Center) covering a total area of 5,101.1 sq. m. are affected by the landslide.

A total of 482 personnel from public health facilities are susceptible to hazards brought by a landslide, with Milagrosa Health Center having the highest magnitude of personnel, followed by the Maduya Health Center and Bancal Health Center, with 68 and 44 personnel, respectively.

#### C. HOUSING

- C.1. HOUSING SITUATION In 2011, 19,556 households were occupying 19,127 housing units with a total population of 78,225. The ratio of the household to the

occupied housing unit is 1:1.02, indicating a backlog of 429 housing units while the 2014 CBMS household survey report showed a backlog of 329 housing units. By 2017, there are 24,322 housing units occupied by 24, 338 households with a total of 27, 467 families. The ratio of the household to the occupied housing unit is 1:1, indicating a backlog of 2,288 housing units.

- C.2. HOUSING BACKLOG

Determining the total backlog is done by adding the doubled-up households, unacceptable housing units, and the makeshift/salvaged/improvised housing units. The unacceptable housing units around .65% is made up of light and mixed materials and estimated lost due to obsolescence and annual decay. The total backlog is 2,288 housing units. The tenure status of occupied housing units are also considered, including those who own or rent-free house and lots without the consent of owner and are living in a public space with or without rent.

![image 735](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile735.png>)

- C.3. INFORMAL SETTLEMENT AREAS In 2017, there are 757 households identified as Informal Settlers (IS). They are those residing along riverbanks, easements, under the

bridges, flood-prone areas, along fault lines and other critical zones. This number represents 3.11% of the total households.

Barangay Bancal got the highest magnitude with 459 households, located in Purok Agiit, Carillo Drive, Eureka, Abandon Road Area, Corehouse, and Governor's Drive. Cabilang Baybay and Maduya followed with 94 and 87 households, respectively.

On the other hand, the least magnitude of informal settlers came from Barangay Mabuhay with 16 reported households. Through the Relocation Program with Integrated Social Services (PUROK KALINGA) for informal settlers initiated by the local government in 2012, the LGU was able to decrease the number of informal settlers from 4.21% (921 IS) in 2011 to 3.11% (757 IS) in 2017.

![image 739](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile739.png>)

The updated list of informal settlers per barangay identified 2,345 Informal Settler Families (ISFs) in the municipality. This includes the total informal settlers (757) reported in 2017. About 1,358 families are living in government-owned lots. It is the highest number among the seven categories. Those 1,358 families are allowed to use the lots unless the need for government development arises. It is also notable that families living in landslide and flood-prone areas (Category 2 and 3) are exposed to public health and safety harm, along with 313 families living along river banks.

The highest frequency of ISFs classified as Categories 1, 2, and 5 occurred in the most populated barangay, Barangay 12-Milagrosa (Phase 4), and one of the relocation sites in Carmona. Meanwhile, Category 6 or those ISFs living in privately-owned lots occupied the second-largest composition (398), followed by the 313 families living along river banks. Furthermore, Barangay 14-Bancal charted the highest number of ISFs living in vacant lots (Category 7), followed by 80 ISFs in Maduya Proper.

- C.4. INVENTORY OF RESIDENTIAL SUBDIVISIONS As of 2019, the municipality has twenty-three (23) residential subdivisions with a total land area of 234.45 hectares. Two (2) of these are

Socialized Housing, where space requirements and cost per unit are affordable to low-income families. These include the Paligawang Matanda Relocation Project and the Milagrosa Homes. The remaining twenty-one (21) are either the open market or economic type, which caters to medium to high-income families in and outside of the municipality. With an aggregate area of 234 hectares and 13,972 housing units, the density is 46 housing units per hectare.

- C.5. RESETTLEMENT AREAS Paligawan and Milagrosa Homes are the relocation sites for informal settler families (ISFs) in Carmona. In 2019, 315 out of 358 lots at

Paligawan were successfully turned-over to ISFs, while 120 lots out of the 131 lots available for displacements awarded to ISFs at Milagrosa Homes. Fifteen (15) families were also displaced to Cabilang Baybay Relocation Site.

Other relocation programs and activities of the LGU include the completion of the Cabilang Baybay Relocation Site, Tenement Housing at Lot 1196 and Catarining Properties, expansion of the Paligawan Relocation Site, and the on-going meetings with Yao Villas Homeowners regarding the acquisition of Yao property for distribution of lots to 191 ISFs.

In 2017, Carmona had 24,338 housing units with 11, 422 households (46.96%) own their house and lot, while 8,283 or 34.06% are renting either a house or a room. Others own the house but are renting the lots on some kinds of arrangements. The rest of the household population is either living in public places; with rented/owned house or lot, or rent-free house or lot without consent of the owner. These households fall under the informal settler’s category and are usually the problem area of the housing sector. The need to relocate to a more livable site is always the priority for this category of a household.

The Socialized Housing Project of the local government will prioritize Informal Settler Families (ISFs), while those individuals or families belonging to doubled-up households will come after.

At present, the Municipality of Carmona, Cavite has six (6) potential lands for housing development, including Lot 1196 at Barangay Milagrosa, which measures 2.7 hectares of land owned by the municipality of Carmona. Other potential lots for housing development are Anahaw Agapito and Catarining located at Barangay Mabuhay, with the total undeveloped land areas of 1.5 hectares and 1.4 hectares, respectively. The undeveloped land owned by De Mata at Barangay Milagrosa will be instigated for the re-development of Phase 4, which is one of the resettlement areas in the municipality.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (RESIDENTIAL)

###### A. RESIDENTIAL AREAS AT RISK TO FLOODING

Based on disaster risk assessment (urban use), two barangays (Maduya and Cabilang Baybay) are susceptible to moderate flooding that has a frequent (every 1 to 3 years) likelihood of occurrence, and high flooding every 4 to 10 years, affecting a total residential area of 9.96 hectares. Barangays with moderate flooding susceptibility have less than or equal to 1m flood depth, while barangays with high flood susceptibility have greater than or equal to 1m flood depth. Although the proposed land use category was residential, the specific uses have been identified as industrial in actuality, particularly in Barangay Maduya, and legal easement in Cabilang Baybay, wherein residential houses are present. The total affected population in Barangay Maduya is 10,551 or 2,942 households, while 7,120 individuals or 1,869 households are vulnerable to flooding hazards in Barangay Cabilang Baybay.

###### B. RESIDENTIAL AREAS AT RISK TO LANDSLIDE

With the occasional landslide that is possible to hit the municipality, the residential areas from all barangays covering a total area of 305.65 hectares are susceptible to landslides with low-risk levels. Furthermore, based on the approved land use category, the following are residential areas in the municipality; however, the specific uses are commercial, industrial, agricultural, legal easement, and Local Water District, where some families reside. The total affected population is 95,338 or 24,338 households. The highest magnitude is situated in barangays Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal.

Furthermore, residential areas in Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal are susceptible to moderate and high landslides. About 74,511 individuals or 18,479 households will be affected by moderate and high landslides. However, a total of 0.81 hectares of residential areas in Barangays Mabuhay and Bancal are susceptible to a very high landslide, affecting a total population of 23,877 or 6,524 households. These barangays have occasional landslide occurrence as well that made the risk category fall under the low level.

#### D. SOCIAL WELFARE SERVICES D.1. SOCIAL WELFARE FACILITIES AND SERVICES OFFERED

In 2019, the municipality had six (6) social welfare organizations, including LCPC, BCPC, Parent’s Association Committee (PAC), Office of the Senior Citizens Affairs, Technical Working Group (TWG) of Project TIKLOP, and Children’s Group in every Barangay. The type of Social Services offered in all barangays is Family Life Education and Counseling, Family Planning Assistance and Day Care Services, and Supplemental Feeding Program under the Parents’ Association Committee. TIKLOP Project also offers social services like Medical Care, Relief/Rehabilitation, and Livelihood Program.

served by the MSWD. In 2019, the Municipal Social Welfare and Development-Carmona served 10,216 clienteles from different disadvantaged groups. It is 73.67% lower than the previous year’s 38,804 clienteles. Most of the underprivileged communities are situated at Barangay Milagrosa (Phase 3 ½ and Phase 4), who had been the beneficiaries of various LGU programs such as Pamaskong Handog and Displaced Families.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (CHILD DEVELOPMENT CENTER AND EVACUATION CENTER)

###### A. CHILD DEVELOPMENT CENTER AND EVACUATION CENTER AT RISK TO LANDSLIDE

All Child Development Centers in the municipality are exposed to landslide with low susceptibility and low-risk level, except Milagrosa Phase 4 Child Development Center in Barangay Milagrosa with high susceptibility and moderate landslide susceptibility at Paligawan and Davilan Child Development Center in Barangay Lantic. These critical facilities have a very low vulnerability to the impact of the landslide, affecting a total area of 1,052.1 sq. m. Furthermore, the CARSIGMA Evacuation Center is also susceptible to a low-risk level of a landslide, affecting its total land area of 4,232.2 sq. m. The occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard made these critical facilities fall under a low-risk category.

About 1,116 pupils from various Child Development Centers are exposed to landslide hazards at a low-risk category since there is an occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard. Child Development Center in Barangay Bancal got the highest magnitude of pupils affected with 158, followed by Cabilang Baybay (132), Lantic (125), Milagrosa Phase 3 (105), and Mabuhay with 100 pupils.

- E. PROTECTIVE SERVICES As of 2019, Carmona has a total of 127 protective personnel: 83 in the police force, 28 in fire protection and 16 in jail management. Currently, there

are six (6) Police Stations and two (2) Fire Stations strategically located at different barangays. The main police station and fire station is situated at the DILG Building in Barangay Maduya.

The Philippine National Police (PNP)-Carmona is responsible for the maintenance of peace and order in the municipality. The total crime volume of the municipality was 96, with a crime solution efficiency rate of 77.08%. With the current police to population ratio of 1: 1,236, the municipality is below the standard ratio of 1:1,000. With this, the agency requires additional police personnel to accommodate the increasing number of populations.

- E.4. CRIME INCIDENCE BY BARANGAY FOR THE LAST FIVE YEARS The crime incidences for the past five years showed a decreasing trend. The lowest crime rate registered in five years was in

2019 at 7.71%, while the highest incidence recorded was 15.04% in 2015. Moreover, the highest crime solution efficiency was also recorded in 2019, with 77.89%.

Barangay Bancal has the highest number of crime incidents (24), followed by Barangay Mabuhay (18), and Barangay Maduya (16). The highest incident reported in 2019 is the violation of RA 9165, also known as the Comprehensive Drugs Act of 2002, with 57 cases. The composition of crime incidents in the Poblacion area includes violations against the Republic Act 9165, Republic Act 9262, or the Anti-Violence Against Women and their Children Act of 2004, theft, and rape. Barangays with the large population density have a

![image 918](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile918.png>)

high contribution to drug-related activities in the municipality. It is evident at Barangay Bancal (20), Maduya (11), and Mabuhay (10) reported incidents. Furthermore, Barangay Milagrosa recorded two (2) incidents involving women abuse or the violation of RA 9262, also known as the Anti-Violence Against Women and the Children Act of 2004. (Refer to Annex A. Crime Incidence by Sex for the detailed information of crime incidence).

- E.5. CRIME INCIDENCE FOR CHILDREN (BELOW 18 YEARS OLD) For the last five (5) years, the Municipality of Carmona, Cavite has reported a total of four (4) crime incidents involving children who are

below 18 years old, also known as Children in Conflict with the Law (CICL). The table above shows the current and projected need for police and fire personnel in the municipality of Carmona. Currently, there are twenty-five

(25) policemen needed by the Carmona Municipal Police Station (MPS) to meet the standard police to population ratio of 1: 1,000.

The Bureau of Fire Protection (BFP)-Carmona, on the other hand, requires twenty-three (23) firefighters to meet the standard fireman to population ratio of 1: 2,000. Thus, Carmona MPS and BFP will require additional personnel for the following years due to the current and projected increase in volume of the population.

![image 930](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile930.png>)

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (PROTECTIVE SERVICES SUB-SECTOR)

###### A. PROTECTIVE SERVICES FACILITIES AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE

Three of the major protective services facilities, namely, the Bureau of Fire Protection (BFP), Bureau of Jail Management and Penology (BJMP) and the Carmona Municipal Police Station at Barangay Maduya with a total land area of 894.3 sq. m. are exposed to landslide with low susceptibility and low risk level. Due to occasional likelihood of landslide occurrence, these three (3) protective services facilities have low risk category, affecting 121 personnel. Of the affected population, 22 are BFP personnel, 16 are BJMP personnel and 83 are policemen.

#### F. SPORTS AND RECREATION

- F. 1. EXISTING SPORTS AND RECREATIONAL FACILITIES The major sports and recreational destinations in Carmona are the Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club in Brgy. Cabilang Baybay, the

Paragliding Site, Ahn’s Baseball Academy, San Lazaro Leisure Park and Casino Filipino in Barangay Lantic, Carmona Racing Circuit in Barangay Bancal, Carmona Cavite Integrated Shooters and Viewfort situated in Barangay Mabuhay. At present, Carmona has two Municipal Parks located at Barangay Lantic and Cabilang Baybay. A newly constructed playground is available at Barangay Cabilang Baybay beside the Carmona Community Center (C3).

There are 14 public and eight (8) private playgrounds in the municipality. Most of these playgrounds are located in public elementary schools, while others are on the premises of private residential subdivisions. Another commonly used sports and recreation facilities are basketball courts. Out of 44 basketball courts in the municipality, 22 are privately owned, found in private residential subdivisions, private schools, and companies, while 22 are for public use maintained by the LGU, barangays, and public schools.

Moreover, there are eleven (11) swimming pools located in private residential subdivisions and a public swimming pool maintained by the municipal government at Barangay Lantic. The tennis court in Cityland Subdivision is also available in Barangay Mabuhay.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (PARKS AND RECREATION)

The CDRA results for parks and recreation shows that five facilities are affected by exposure to landslide. These include one of the tourism facilities in the municipality, the Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club at Cabilang Baybay, two clubhouses (Villa Alegre and Cityland Clubhouse) in Barangay Mabuhay, Municipal Park at Barangay Lantic and the Town Plaza at Barangay 4. The likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional or is bound to experience every 11-30 years, making the risk level fall under the low category.

facilities risk assessment, a total of 123 facilities are exposed to different categories of landslide susceptibility. All barangays are exposed to landslide with low to high susceptibility, and low risk level. Occasional landslide is possible in the municipality, affecting the Carmona Public Market, Municipal Trial Court, 14 barangay halls, 1 Evacuation Center, 1 Fire Station, 5 Government Offices, 13 Health Centers, 2 Hospitals, 1 Police Station, 12 Public Schools, 1 Cemetery, 10 Communication Towers, 12 Child Development Centers, 1 Jail Facility, 32 Fire Hydrants and 16 Pumping Stations. ### Chapter 5: ECONOMIC SECTOR

#### A. AGRICULTURE

- A.1. EXISTING MAJOR AGRICULTURE CROPS In 2019, the 285.1 hectares planted with rice produced 1,143.55 metric tons. Based on the required per capita of 0.124, this is significantly lower than

11,361 metric tons. Likewise, the production of rice and vegetables decreased due to the conversion of agricultural land to other land uses, but significant portions are no longer arable due to climate change and the lack of water resources. The municipality is now looking at mitigating measures as well as alternative measures of the use of land.

Rice planting in the two rice producing barangays of the municipality peaks during March, July, and October, when water for irrigation is sufficient. Harvesting in Barangay Maduya and Lantic was at its highest yield during February, July, October, and November when weather conditions are favorable.

Total production volume is about 1,143.55 metric tons valued at Php 19, 440,350.00 harvested from 245.3 hectares of land. Various vegetable and fruit seedlings were distributed to farmers of Barangays Lantic, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, and Bancal under the BALIK BINHI Program in 2019. Additional agricultural support facilities were also constructed, including the 1,110 meters of irrigation canal at Brgy. Lantic, Carmona, by the Local Government of Carmona.

![image 1050](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1050.png>)

As of 2019, there are six crops produced at different barangays. The highest volume produced was noted for irrigated rice, with 1, 143.55 metric tons amounting to 19,440,350.00, followed by corn with 474.4 metric tons, at a value of 4,744,000.

Meanwhile, permanent crops like mango and banana got the least production volume this year. Mango has only 264.5 metric tons with a value of 6,612,500.00, while the banana has 121.23 metric tons produced in the amount of 1,454,760.00. Each agricultural crop are primarily being sold locally within the municipality with some heading towards nearby municipalities and provinces.

exist in the municipality wherein rice-irrigated remains to have the highest volume produced, while banana got the least production volume in 2019. From the table above, the production area for corn increased to 47.5 hectares in 2019 from 23.5 hectares in 2017.

###### A.4. WATER IRRIGATION SYSTEMS

###### A. Irrigation Pump Currently, the municipality has six (6) irrigation pumps and three (3) dams namely, Prinsang Luma (Lantic 1 Dam); Prinsang Bago (Lantic 2 Dam);

and Prinsa Lantic 3 Dam strategically located at Barangay Lantic. Two (2) of these pumps were being operated through the use of diesel while the other four (4) are electrically operated. These pumps efficiencies have been reduced over time due to climate-induced droughts.

![image 1066](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1066.png>)

In the forthcoming, these irrigation pumps can be turned over to the Carmona Water District as additional source of potable water supply. Green areas of the municipality will be more beneficial in the future through non-agricultural development. At present, the Local Government is carrying the burden of costly agricultural production, which is way higher than the income from its harvests.

Incontestably, Agriculture plays a crucial role for most of the farmers in the locality. However, a strong inclination of successors to farming is rare. Second, big developers owned vast agricultural lands and have no intention to till them. Lastly, limited available land resources in Carmona necessitates sage maximization of its land. Hence, it is vital to ensure the sustainability of growth and development in the municipality by not undermining the profitable uses of the remaining agricultural land.

![image 1067](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1067.png>)

###### Map 5-1. Location of Irrigation Pump

![image 1068](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1068.png>)

- A.5. AGRICULTURAL OCCUPATIONS/GROUPS IN URBAN AND RURAL AREAS There are 1,761 rural workers composed of rice farmers, crop farmers, livestock farmers, and poultry farmers in 2019. It is significantly lower

compared to the previous record of 1,899 rural farmers working at the major and minor occupation. Minor occupation group, which includes rice and crop farmers are mostly from Barangay Maduya, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal. The major occupation group includes livestock farmers located at Barangays 8-14 and poultry farmers at fourteen (14) barangays.

| |Lantic| |31|31|

| |Lantic| |63|63|

|Livestock Farmers|8-14| |819|819|

|Total| | |1,761|1,761|

Source: DA

Agriculture plays a crucial role for most of the farmers in the locality. In reality, many have accomplished lives through farming, depicted through the educational attainment of their lineage. However, in recent years, steered by the advancement of technology, the strong inclination of successors to agriculture is rare. From this perception comes the need to convert the remaining agricultural land to commercial land use for practical reasons.

First, the local government, at present, is carrying the burden of costly agriculture production. Specifically, the operation and maintenance of farm machinery varying from pre and post-harvest of rice production, and the water irrigation. Furthermore, the production value is higher compared to the total value of rice harvested. Thus, converting agricultural land for development use will be profitable and helpful for the local government to further its advancement in becoming the next “Smart City” in the South.

![image 1072](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1072.png>)

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT: AGRICULTURE

- A. AGRICULTURAL LANDS AFFECTED BY FLOODING Based on the climate and disaster risk assessment (natural resource-based production area), Barangays Maduya, Cabilang Baybay and Bancal are

susceptible to moderate and high flooding with moderate risk levels. The total agricultural land affected is 4.49 hectares and are currently utilized as rice fields, commercial and industrial establishments. Moderate flood susceptibility in Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay has less than or equal to 1m flood depth while high flood susceptibility has greater than or equal to 1m flood depth. Furthermore, since the likelihood of occurrence is moderate, those three barangays being mentioned will expect to experience this type of hazard every 4-10 years.

- B. AGRICULTURAL LANDS AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE Agricultural land areas at Barangays 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, and 14 are susceptible to low landslides with low-risk levels. A total area of 494.99 hectares

with the specific use of commercial, industrial, residential, and the remaining portion solely for agricultural activities are affected by the landslide at a lowrisk level.

The disaster risk assessment (NRBPA) also shows Barangays Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal have moderate landslide susceptibility. The total agricultural lands affected are 278.95 hectares with low-risk levels due to its improbable likelihood of occurrence (every 31-100 years).

High landslide susceptibility is also present with moderate risk category at Barangays 10, 11, 13, and 14, and low-risk category at Barangays 11 to 14. The total affected area for barangays susceptible to high landslide with moderate risk levels is 654.81 hectares, while 9.72 hectares for the low-risk category. Furthermore, Barangay Bancal has very high exposure to landslides with a low-risk level. Since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is improbable, the risk category level is low and moderate.

#### AGRICULTURE ANALYSIS MATRIX

|OBSERVED CONDITION/ISSUES|EXPLANATION (CAUSES)|IMPLICATIONS WHEN UNRESOLVED|STRATEGIES/INTERVENTION/POLICY OPTIONS<br><br>|
|---|---|---|---|
|Decreasing rice agricultural output|Reduction in natural water resources|Reduced harvests overtime, increasing cost of agriculture produce especially rice| Explore alternative use of Riceland<br> Explore urban and rooftop farming with rain catchers<br>|
|Inadequate manpower on agricultural operations|Decreasing trained farm operators|Reduced harvests overtime| Increase automation by introducing machines to support the farmers<br> Promote farming as a viable career for young people<br>|
|High cost of agricultural production|High electricity cost of irrigation pumps which are currently used in the municipality’s agriculture; Increasing prices of farm inputs and fuel|Reduced harvests overtime, increasing cost of agriculture produce especially rice| Explore alternative use of Riceland which are reliant on expensive pump irrigation<br> Explore urban and rooftop farming with rain catchers<br>|
|Inadequate capacity development/ training of DA Staff|Limited personnel|Inefficient delivery of services to clients pertaining to proper pet handling and vaccination; Increase in number of animal bite cases| Training/ Seminar on proper pet handling and vaccination|
|Agricultural land in Barangays Maduya, Cabilang Baybay and Bancal are susceptible to moderate to high flooding affecting 4.40 hectares with moderate risk level|Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal were traversed by the Carmona River and theses agricultural lands were situated near the river and its tributaries and the likelihood of occurrence is 4-10 years|Damage to crops; Increase in the cost of LGU to provide irrigation infrastructures and procurement of crops, seedlings, fertilizer, and pesticides| Sourcing/Purchasing of climatechange crops and seedlings<br> Sourcing/Purchasing of fertilizer and pesticides climate changes<br>|

- B. COMMERCE AND TRADE Commerce and trade are also known as the tertiary sector or the service and industry sector, which provides services on trade, commerce, and

retailing. Known as the investment hub in the Province of Cavite, Carmona has become the top choice of foreign and local investors through its high-quality infrastructure facilities and business-friendly services. In 2019, a total of 2,724 business establishments were registered; 2,405 of which are commercial and 319 are industrial. Of the twelve (12) existing Industrial Estates, 485 are registered locators and seven (7) are operating under the Philippine Economic Zone Authority (PEZA).

The majority of registered business establishments are in retail, services, and manufacturing sectors. Among those industrial subdivisions, Golden Mile Business Park has the most number of companies with 58, followed by the People’s Technology Complex with 48, and Mount View I Industrial Complex with 33 companies.

- B.1. FINANCIAL INSTITUTIONS There are 69 financial institutions broken down as follows: Twenty (20) banks, fourteen (14) pawnshops, nine (9) lending institutions, sixteen

(16) multi-purpose Cooperative, and ten (10) other financial institutions. Barangay Maduya occupies the highest percentage of financial institutions due to its strategic location.

- B.2. WHOLESALE AND RETAIL TRADE The wholesale trade sector comprises establishments engaged in wholesaling merchandise, generally without transformation and rendering

services incidental to the sale of merchandise. The wholesaling process is an intermediate step in the distribution of merchandise.

The retail trade sector comprises establishments engaged in retailing merchandise, generally without transformation and rendering services incidental to the sale of merchandise. The retailing process is the final step in the distribution of merchandise, wherein retailers are organized to sell merchandise in small quantities to the general public. This sector comprises two main types of retailers: store and non-store retailers. Trading activity is the major line of business in Carmona which accounts to 995 wholesalers and retailers for the year 2019.

![image 1108](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1108.png>)

- B.3. PERSONAL SERVICES Service enterprises are organizations that facilitate the production and distribution of goods, support other firms in meeting their goals, and add

value to our personal lives. As of 2019, there are 243 establishments engaged in the community, social and personal services in the municipality. Personal services included 14 barbershops; 13 beauty parlors; 3 funeral parlors; 15 spas; 6 Gyms; 15 laundry shops; 4 photo/video shops; 49 refilling stations; 13 tailoring/dress shops; 21 printing shops; 39 computer rental/internet café; 22 Repair Services; and 29 Construction Services.

The Municipality of Carmona, through the Municipal Social Welfare and Development Office (MSWDO) and in partnership with the Department of Science and Technology (DOST), provides various livelihood and skills training programs to entrepreneurs. Financial assistance is likewise extended, for Small and Medium Enterprise (SME) development.

- B.5. HISTORICAL DATA ON COMMERCIAL ESTABLISHMENTS BY CATEGORY In 2019, the municipality had 2,405 commercial establishments. These establishments include different types of businesses such as wholesale trade

and retail, hotel and restaurant transport and storage, communication establishments, financial intermediation, real estate renting and business activities, public admin and defense, education, health and social work, other community, social and personal services.

Barangay Maduya and Mabuhay got the highest number of registered commercial establishments with 682 and 454 establishments, respectively, while the least number is Cabilang Baybay with 105 establishments. Furthermore, Barangay Lantic got the highest rate of increase at 29.49%, followed by Barangay Milagrosa (8.07%), and Barangay Mabuhay (6.82%).

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (COMMERCIAL AREAS)

###### A. COMMERCIAL AREA AFFECTED BY FLOODING

Based on disaster risk assessment, high and moderate flooding susceptibility are present at Barangays Maduya and Bancal, affecting a total commercial area of 4.21 hectares with a total population of 24,795 or 7,126 households. The vulnerability of these barangays to the impact of flooding is very low. The expected flood depth at Bancal and Maduya with high flooding susceptibility is greater than or equal to 1m, while Barangay Maduya with moderate flooding susceptibility has a flood depth that is less than or equal to 1m.

#### C. INDUSTRY

- C.1. HISTORICAL DATA ON INDUSTRIES There are twelve (12) existing industrial estates and 4 industrial compounds in the Municipality with 319 registered industrial companies in 2019.

This is higher by 22.69% compared to the previous record. Companies outside these industrial estates and compound can be found along the stretch of Governor’s Drive.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT: INDUSTRIAL AREAS

- A. INDUSTRIAL AREA AFFECTED BY FLOODING Industrial areas in Barangay Maduya and Bancal are exposed to moderate and high flood susceptibility with moderate-risk levels. The urban use risk

assessment shows that moderate flood will be frequently (every 1-3 years) experienced by the affected industrial area in Barangay Maduya. Meanwhile, high flood susceptibility will occur at a moderate level (every 4-10 years), particularly at Barangay Bancal and Maduya, with a total of 1.55 hectares of land affected. The expected flood depth for moderate flood susceptibility is less than or equal to 1-meter, while greater than or equal to 1-meter flood depth is present at Barangays Maduya and Bancal with high flooding susceptibility. There are 24,795 persons or 7,126 households affected, of which 14,244 belong to Barangay Bancal, while 10,551 persons belong to Barangay Maduya. These two barangays have the most number of industrial subdivisions where some families reside.

- B. INDUSTRIAL AREA AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE In terms of landslide susceptibility, a total area of 335.19 hectares of industrial lands from Barangay Rosario, Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay,

Lantic, and Bancal is affected by a low landslide. The total affected population is 64,271 or 16,666 households, of which Barangay Lantic and Bancal got the highest number exposed to landslide hazards. On the other hand, industrial areas with moderate landslide susceptibility are identified at Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal, affecting 42.58 hectares. The total population exposed to moderate landslide is 50,694 or 12,866 households. Generally, the risk level is low since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is occasional (every 4-10 years).

#### INDUSTRY ANALYSIS MATRIX

|OBSERVED CONDITION/ISSUES|EXPLANATION (CAUSES)|IMPLICATIONS WHEN UNRESOLVED|STRATEGIES/INTERVENTION/POLICY OPTIONS<br><br>|
|---|---|---|---|
|Lack of Industrial Mapping and Development System|Lack of point person/department, expertise and system|Delay in providing updated data; inefficient monitoring and formulation of economic development plan<br><br>|Establishment of Industrial Mapping & Information System|
|Lack of centralized local economic database| | |Establishment of Local Economic Database|
|Not maximizing the revenue potential of local economic enterprises|Lack of mechanism to maximize the potential of local economic enterprises and LGU-owned properties|Minimal economic return from local economic enterprises| Promotion and Further Development of Local Economic Enterprises to maximize revenue potential<br> Develop LGU-owned land for commercial and industrial lease<br>|
|Weak/delayed information dissemination among all registered businesses|Lack of complete directory of industries (BPLS registry contains incomplete contact details of registered businesses). During pandemic and emergency disasters, only locators inside industrial estates were able to receive information thru industrial estates administrators|Less engagement of the business sector in LGU initiatives and inefficient information dissemination that might cause additional confusion and negative effects| Quarterly dialogue with Locators & Business Stakeholders<br> Online dissemination of information through Website, Twitter, Facebook etc<br>|
|Low count of members of Carmona Business Club as compared to number of registered businesses|No increase in members among Carmona Business Club for several years; not all locators are members|No increase or possible decrease in members of CBC; Unmaximized potential of CBC in contributing to economic development| Revitalizing Carmona Business Club through events and roadshows|
|Lack of industrial estates administrators association; locators association in industrial compounds|Local industrial estates and compounds association are not unified|Uninformed stakeholders and less engagement of business community for LGU objectives| Organization of Carmona Industrial Estates Association<br> Organize association of locators per Industrial compound<br> Establishment of Association of Carmona Entrepreneurs<br>|

- D.6. CULTURAL AND TOURISM ACTIVITIES/ FESTIVALS Preserving its culture, Carmona celebrates its Sorteo Festival every three

years offering a weeklong festivity which highlights the unique tradition of lottery of farm lands to the natives of the town. Carmona is also a pool of cultural wealth with their number of Marching Bands and the people’s inclination to performing arts. With this, the local government hosted events and competitions of all sorts which are included in the Weeklong Celebration of Sorteo ng Bukid Festival, Carmona Day and Carmona Town Fiesta.

![image 1253](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1253.png>)

###### D.7. POTENTIAL TOURIST ATTRACTIONS IN THE LOCALITY

The Municipality, through the MITCAO and the LEIPO shall strategize to encourage the entry of more investment in tourism, event, and leisureoriented businesses and excellent business and luxury hotels. Some of the potential tourist attractions are street murals, Balayong and Banaba Tree Park, Botanical Garden with Koi ponds and lantern, Los Angeles County Museum of Art (LACMA) local version, and Kyoto’s Kimono Forest Local version showcasing Philippine fabrics.

![image 1254](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1254.png>)

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (TOURISM FACILITIES)

- A. TOURISM FACILITIES AFFECTED BY FLOODING Based on urban use disaster risk assessment, the 4.95 hectares of Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club (MSWGCC) at Barangay Cabilang

Baybay is exposed to moderate and high flood susceptibility with a very low vulnerability. The risk category for this tourism facility is moderate since this hazard occurs every 4-10 years. The expected flood depth for the moderate flooding susceptibility is less than or equal to 1m while high flooding susceptibility has greater than or equal to 1m flood depth.

- B. TOURISM FACILITIES AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE The municipality has an occasional likelihood of occurrence for landslide hazard, affecting all barangays at a low-risk level and very low

vulnerability. Moderate landslide is present at five barangays, affecting 121.43 hectares of commercial, industrial and residential land. Tourism facilities such as Firing Range in Barangay Milagrosa, the Manila Jockey Club in Barangay Lantic, and the Manila Southwoods Golf and Country Club in Cabilang Baybay are also susceptible to moderate landslide with low-risk level. The total affected area is 46.34 hectares, which has very low vulnerability to the impact of a landslide. Generally, the affected tourism facilities have low-risk levels to landslide hazards since the likelihood of occurrence is occasional (every 11 to 30 years).

|---|---|---|---|
|TOURISM| | | |
|Stagnant local tourism industry|Absence of strategic action plan for local tourism development|Visitor arrival and tourism earnings may decline.|Approve and execute a strategic Local Tourism Development Plan with the assistance from the Department of Tourism (DOT) and MPDC|
|Primary Tourism Enterprises are not DOT-accredited|Lack of programs and policies supporting primary tourism enterprises in ensuring compliance to DOT standards|The quality of facilities and services are not in line with the DOT’s standard which may affect the satisfaction of tourists and support from DOT|Orient and equip tourism establishments with capacity and knowledge they need to comply with Department of Tourism|
|Absence of local tourism destinations for the general public|Non-existence of man-made tourism sites such as street murals, additional parks, and public installation arts in strategic locations|Lower Gross Domestic Happiness level, which is one of the priority thrusts of the municipality|Establish small scale local tourist attractions in Carmona|
|Lack of knowledge of local products by local community and the broader market|Absence of programs and distribution channel to promote the local products of the municipality|Local products of Carmona will not be promoted, and earnings of local producers will remain stagnant or even decline.|Create programs promoting local products of the municipality|
|Unregulated tourist activities in tourism destinations such as the Carmona Paragliding Site|Absence of resolution regulating the activities in the Carmona Paragliding Site|The safety and the quality of service may be compromised resulting in low visitor turnout.|Provide resolution regulating the paragliding activities in the Carmona Paragliding Site and training to its personnel.|
|CULTURE AND ARTS| | | |
|Absence of database/ inventory of cultural heritage and properties in Carmona|Lack of in-depth study on Carmona’s history, culture and heritage|Cultural heritage and properties that are undetermined or unrecognized will be irretrievable once resources decline.<br><br>LGU will fail to comply with the National Cultural Heritage Act of 2009.|Conduct a cultural mapping in coordination with the National Commission for Culture and the Arts (NCCA) and the MPDC|

|---|---|---|---|
|Absence of projects that showcase, promote and preserve the cultural heritage and properties of Carmona|Lack of technical knowledge and skills related to the promotion and preservation of cultural heritage and properties|The absence of a comprehensive report/narrative about Carmona’s cultural heritage and properties (including the Sorteo Festival) would contribute to the continuous decline in resources, resulting in the difficulties or impossibility in the recollection and retrieval of the municipality’s identity| Produce a Coffee Table Book and Audio-Visual Presentation based on the findings and results of the cultural mapping<br> Install a community museum that will promote and preserve Carmona’s history, culture and heritage for locals and visitors in coordination with the Municipal Engineering Office<br>|

### Chapter 6 : INFRASTRUCTURE SECTOR

- A. TRANSPORTATION Carmona is accessible thru land travel from all directions. It is at the crossroads from north to south between Cavite and Laguna as well as the

gateway to the southern provinces of the region and beyond. From Carmona Public Market Terminal, Jeepney routes from Carmona to Biñan and GMA/Dasmariñas are available via the provincial road Governor’s Drive and the National Highway. Bus and Jeepney trips to Alabang, Manila and Calamba are available going thru the South Expressway and Biñan thru the national roads. San Pedro, Laguna is also accessible thru Southwoods Drive in Barangay Cabilang-Baybay while the inner barangays of Biñan, Laguna, Laguna Technopark and Laguna International Industrial Park (LIIP) may be accessed thru Dahlia Street and Mayor’s Boulevard. Tagaytay is accessed thru Maguyam Road which connects to Aguinaldo Highway in Silang, Cavite.

The average travel time within a 30-kilometer zone is about 30 minutes without traffic. Increasing traffic volume has made trips in all direction longer than the normal travel time due to the limited carrying capacity of the major road systems. New access points to and from the municipality is a priority and several old roads have been improved while new ones are laid to provide additional access. The old Sugar road is a good choice for a new north-south access via Southwoods Drive and South Expressway to Sta. Rosa and Canlubang via Loma in Biñan, Laguna. The rough road of Paligawan is also an ideal route going to Silang and Tagaytay City if it could be connected to Sugar road and/or Mayor’s Boulevard. Another South Express exit linking Mayor’s Boulevard is another alternative considered to ease the huge traffic at the Carmona South Luzon Expressway (SLEX) toll exit during rush hours. Currently, in 2020, the current interchange has a total average daily interchange traffic of 34,820 vehicles per day, and it is estimated that by 2040 it will be 127,591 as the municipality and the municipalities and cities around Carmona develop.

As Carmona is a major commuter artery to and from Cavite, Biñan, Laguna, new commuter infrastructure is being explored to increase service quality, reduce road congestion, and increase economic development for the municipality. Digitizing the transport layer through technology that will help route planning, estimated times, and infrastructure development is a focus of the municipality.

![image 1304](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1304.png>)

Local transportation is also a priority of the municipality, with a focus on encouraging the use of bikes and walking, possibly through mixed-use development. Electric vehicle infrastructure is also going to be promoted with new and current developments to reduce carbon dioxide and other toxic fumes and the level of noise pollution in the municipality.

- A.1. ROADS In 2019, the Municipality of Carmona, Cavite, had a total of 159.99 kilometers of road. Of this 159.99 km, 12.71 km are classified as

national/provincial road, 10.86 km of municipal road, and 46.61 km of barangay roads. The remaining 89.81 km are streets comprising alleyways, path walks, private right-of-ways, and subdivisions that serve as the access roads outside government jurisdictions. (Refer to Annex B for the detailed inventory of Roads by System Classification and Type of Pavement)

The type of surface for this road inventory includes concrete, asphalt, and earth/gravel. It has 138.13 km of concrete roads, 8.79 km of asphalt, and 13.07 km of gravel and earth. Using the standard for Urban Road Requirement, which is 2.4 km per 1000 population, the municipality still needs an additional 86.28 km of asphalt road.

Almost 90% of Carmona has now been urbanized, due to the small size of the municipality as well as the rapid development of large residential and commercial subdivisions in Barangays Mabuhay, Lantic, and Bancal. With the carrying capacity of the existing road network being near on its limits, new roads are needed to relieve the pressure of increasing traffic volume.

The biggest road infrastructure issue that Carmona is currently facing is that several portions of Governor’ Drive in Carmona is already beyond capacity with the V/C Ratio of Governor’s Drive at the exit/entrance of SLEX is already at 1.12 by 2040 without major investment in transportation infrastructure the V/C Ratio of Governor’s Drive will be between 1.32 to 2.77 which is truly heavy congestion. Dahlia Street by 2040 will have a V/C Ratio of 1.12 and Mayor’s boulevard is at 0.87 V/C Ratio, and more worryingly Purificacion Street will have a V/C Ratio of 1.13. Governor’s Drive heading to GMA and Dasmariñas will have a V/C Ratio will have 1.32, by 2040 almost all the major roads in Carmona will be have a V/C Ratio of 1 or more where the demand exceeds available capacity of the road.

- b. Inventory of Bridges by Location, Type, Capacity, and Condition The Municipality of Carmona, Cavite has a total of 21 major and minor bridges comprising 4 national bridges, 2 municipal bridges, 4 barangay

bridges, 5-foot bridges and 6 privately-owned bridges inside subdivisions for internal access. Fourteen (14) of these bridges are made of reinforced concrete while the rest are both steel and asphalt or pure steel. The conditions of these bridges are also listed to which thirteen (13) of these are well-maintained or under good condition and are being used for daily transportation. Five (5) bridges are somehow fair while the remaining three (3) bridges are structurally deficient or in poor condition namely, Cityland Mabuhay Mini-Bridge, Altarez-Calabuso Bridge and Bancal-GMA Bridge.

- A.3. FLOOD CONTROL AND DRAINAGE FACILITIES Most of the barangays have existing storm drainage systems running along the streets of the municipality. It consists mainly of reinforced concrete

pipes, lined and unlined open canals and reinforced concrete rectangular culvert or the box type canals. Since Poblacion area is prone to flooding due to the natural grade of the area and the lack of planning during the 70’s, 80’s and 90’s developments, the municipality embarked on a comprehensive program of developing a drainage system that can cope with the fast-growing problem of flooding during the late 90’s up to present. The municipality has now two (2) main storm drainage pipes that can handle vast amount of storm water. One is the 245-meter long, 1.5-2 meter-diameter underground pipe along Purificacion Street. It runs from the existing creek in Barangays 7 and 8 draining to the Carmona River. The other drainage pipe is the 120-meter long, 2-meter-diameter underground pipe along Tenedero Street running from Magallanes Street and draining to the existing creek in Barangay 2 which eventually drains to Carmona River.

Initiated flood control projects include the construction of the Drainage System at Phase 2, Brgy. 8, Hebron St., Brgy. Mabuhay and Carmona National High School (CNHS). Also, there has been a rehabilitation/improvement of canal at Mapalad St., Brgy. Mabuhay and construction of Drainage System (Box Culvert) at Brgy. 8. Despite the increase in the inventory of drainages in the municipality, the construction of effective drainage and flood control system is still needed, particularly at Phase 4, Patindig Araw, which has a relatively small size of drainage concrete pipes that resulted to continuous flooding due to clogged drainages.

The total length of the municipality’s drainage system is 146.93 km. of which 6.67 km or 5% is covered with concrete hollow blocks (CHB), 20.48 km or 14% are open CHB, 89.83 km or 61% is reinforced concrete pipe (RCP). However, there are still 29 km of road in some barangays that have no drainage canals especially in parts of Barangays 3, 4, 6, 7 and 10.

Currently, there is a proposal to open new bridges to connect Sugar Road to Lankiwa Road in Biñan as well as a road linking Mag-asawang Mangga in Governor’s Drive to MJC access road. Some of the accomplished road network projects of the LGU are the Farm to Market Road at Lantic that starts at Alfonso Macha to Sugar Road, Access Road with Drainage and Retaining Wall at Sitio Paligawan Matanda, Brgy. Lantic, Rehabilitation of Road from Asphalt to Concrete at Sitio Paligawan Matanda, and Construction of Davilan Road.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (ROADS)

- A. ROADS AFFECTED BY FLOODING About 2,299.41 meters of roads in Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal have a moderate and high susceptibility to flooding. Barangay

Cabilang Baybay has the longest roads exposed to flooding with a moderate risk level, followed by Maduya (581.40 meters) and Bancal (59.04 meters). The expected flood depth for barangays with high flood susceptibility, including Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal, is greater than or equal to 1m. Meanwhile, moderate flood susceptibility (Barangay Maduya and Cabilang Baybay) has a flood depth of less than or equal to 1m. Since the likelihood of occurrence is every 4 to years, the risk category is moderate.

Based on the assumption for the replacement cost of roads, including base failure, the average-cost would be P14, 560,888.50/km. Barangay Cabilang Baybay got the highest exposure percentage to flooding with 96.66%, while Barangay 6 is 100% exposed to landslides. In general, the municipality needs 33.4 million pesos if the affected roads with a total length of 2,299.41 meters are damaged. The implementation of the existing regulations on hazard mitigation and structural design, along with the continuous monitoring of the compliance, helped in the attainment of the reduced magnitude of roads exposed to flooding and landslide.

- B. ROADS AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE Meanwhile, 192,761.12 meters of roads have a low, moderate, and high susceptibility to the landslide. Barangays Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay,

Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal are susceptible to moderate and high landslides, while all barangays are susceptible to a low landslide. The longest length of roads exposed to landslides with a low-risk level is located in Barangay Lantic, with 56,885.89 meters. The likelihood of occurrence is very rare (every <200 years, which made the identified barangays at a low-risk level to landslide hazards.

With the continuous population growth in the Municipality of Carmona, the local government is embarking on numerous projects including but not limited to a brand-new municipal building, a new public market, the construction of Columbarium with Crematorium and Wake Viewing Chapels and an integrated terminal hub called the Carmona Central Station. These projects will boost the local economy as well as provide more services to the public. Additional buildings such as a new BJMP administration building, a new PNP police station and a new DILG building are also being explored. All of these projects will be smartly financed through public-private partnerships, joint-ventures, concessions etc.

#### B. POWER

- B.1. HOUSEHOLDS SERVED WITH ELECTRICITY Meralco serves about 23, 179 HH or 95.3% of the total HH population for their lighting and cooking needs while the rest have no electrical service at

all using only kerosene, LPG or candle for lighting and cooking needs. Most of the 4.70% or 1, 143 households not being serviced by Meralco are either informal settlers or living in temporary housing. They are usually found along river banks in Milagrosa (CTH, phases 2, 3, 3-1/2 & 4), quarry site and Carbon in Mabuhay. Industrial and commercial establishments enjoy a continuous power service due to the improved condition of the power providers’ facilities. Using the data from the 2014 CBMS household survey shows that the average power consumption is 1,521.80 KW per household per year with a total consumption of 32,677.61 MW.

Assuming a 100% household coverage, the total household power needs would be 34,249.63MW. By the year 2020, Carmona is expected to consume about 42,703.23 MW. Carmona doesn’t have any power generators except for back-up generators and solar panels used by industrial and commercial entities. The municipality is looking to promote the use of solar panels on industrial, commercial and residential in order to reduce load on the power grid and increase resiliency of Carmona.

- C. WATER About 19,290 (79.31%) households have access to safe water community system, while 2,028 (8.34%) are sharing subscription of water service with

another household. Other households from Poblacion and other urban areas in Carmona use bottled water for drinking purposes, consisting of about 1, 671 (6.87%) households.

The rest draw water from deep wells that are being owned or being shared among a number of households. However only a small number of households use water from artesian wells, dug/shallow well, river or streams due to financial constraints. Another small percentage gets their water supply from tanker truck peddler and other sources.

![image 1465](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1465.png>)

Carmona Water District is the sole provider of water services in the municipality of Carmona. As of 2019, there are 14,273 active concessionaires from different barangays, of which 13, 491 are residential/government subscribers, while 782 are subscribers from commercial establishments. Residential and government subscribers peaked at Barangays 9-12 and 14. For commercial concessionaires, Barangay 9- Maduya has the greatest number of subscribers.

There are 17 Carmona Water District Pumping Stations that exist in the municipality as of 2019. These are strategically located at Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic, and Bancal. Barangay Bancal has the most number of pumping stations, followed by Barangay Mabuhay and Cabilang Baybay. To further increase the number of subscribers and ensure the accessibility of people to quality and affordable water, low water rate of the Carmona Water District will be initiated by the Local Government Unit of Carmona, Cavite.

On-going bidding for Mabuhay Pumping Station (PS), Site Preparation for Elevated Water Tank in Carmona Elementary School (CES) PS, Purchase of additional four (4) generator sets for Bancal PS-3, Monte Carlo, Cityland PS-3 and Lantic PS, Installation of Steel Bolted Tank in Bancal PS-3 and procurement of additional vehicle (Multi-cab) for Maintenance works are among the development projects of the Carmona Water District as of 2019.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (WATER PIPELINES)

###### A. WATER PIPELINES AFFECTED BY FLOODING

A total of 51.41 meters of water pipelines at Barangay Maduya have a moderate and high susceptibility to flooding. The occurrence of moderate flooding is frequent (every 1 to 3 years), while high flooding will occur every 4-10 years. For this reason, Barangay Maduya has a moderate risk level of flooding hazards. Moreover, the expected flood depth is less than or equal to 1m for moderate flooding and greater than or equal to 1m for high flood susceptibility.

It is assumed that in a worst-case scenario, the total replacement cost for affected water pipelines (flooding) is estimated at P179,935.00, while P160,318,620.00 is expected replacement cost when the landslide hit the affected 45,805.32 meters of water pipelines at various barangays.

###### Table 6-20. Water Pipelines At Risk to Flooding

|Barangay Name|Total Length|Affected Length|Exposure Percentage|Replacement Cost (PhP)<br><br>|Risk Category|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|Barangay 9 - Maduya|1,155.50|51.41|4.45|179,935|Moderate|
|TOTAL|1,155.50|51.41|4.45|179,935|Moderate|

Source: CDRA-Lifeline Exposure Database (GIS Derived)

###### B. WATER PIPELINES AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE

Meanwhile, water pipelines exposed at landslide with low-risk levels are expected at all barangays in Carmona, with a total length of 45,805.32 meters affected. Low landslide susceptibility is expected in all barangays, affecting a total of 43,486.50 meters of water pipelines. Furthermore, about 876.15 meters of water pipelines in three barangays (Mabuhay, Milagrosa, and Bancal) are susceptible to moderate landslide, and high landslide susceptibility will affect a total of 1,442.73 meters of water pipelines in Barangays Mabuhay and Milagrosa. Generally, these barangays have low-risk levels since the likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard is very rare (every >200 years).

The longest lengths of water pipelines affected by a landslide are Barangay Mabuhay and Milagrosa, consisting of 11,214.99 meters and 10,098.07 meters, respectively. Barangay Maduya followed with a total length of 8,906.21 meters of pipelines affected. It is assumed that in a worst-case scenario, the estimated replacement cost for affected water pipelines (flooding) is PhP 179,935.00, and PhP 160,318,620.00 is the expected replacement cost to landslide hazards.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (WATER FACILITIES -PUMPING STATION)

###### A. WATER FACILITIES (PUMPING STATION) AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE

Sixteen (16) pumping stations with a total area of 4,407 sq. m. are affected by possible landslides in the municipality. These are located at Barangays Lantic, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Cabilang Baybay, Maduya, and Bancal. Fourteen of which have low susceptibility, while the remaining two (2) pumping stations situated at Barangay Mabuhay have moderate and high landslide susceptibility. Due to the occasional (every 11-30 years) occurrence of landslide hazards, the affected barangays have a low-risk category.

#### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (RIVERS AND CREEKS)

###### A. RIVERS AND CREEKS AFFECTED BY FLOODING

Based on disaster risk assessment (NRBPA), rivers in Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, and Bancal are susceptible to moderate and high flooding hazards with moderate-risk level. The likelihood of occurrence for this type of vulnerability is every 4-10 years, affecting a total area of 16.16 hectares. Moderate flooding has less than or equal to 1m flood depth, while high flooding can produce a flood depth that is greater than or equal to 1m.

###### B. RIVERS AND CREEKS AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE

About 52.88 hectares of rivers and creeks from ten (10) Barangays are affected by occasional landslides in the municipality. The susceptibility and vulnerability of these barangays to landslides are low. In particular, rivers situated in Barangay San Pablo, Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, Milagrosa, Lantic and Bancal have low susceptibility to landslide along with irrigation canals at Barangay Rosario and Maduya, and creeks at various barangays (6, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12 and 13). The affected barangays have a low-risk level due to the occasional likelihood of occurrence for this type of hazard.

#### WATER ANALYSIS MATRIX

|OBSERVED CONDITION/ ISSUES|EXPLANATION (CAUSES)|IMPLICATIONS WHEN UNRESOLVED<br><br>|STRATEGIES/ INTERVENTION/ POLICY OPTIONS<br><br>|
|---|---|---|---|
|Meter inaccuracies due to uncalibrated water meters<br><br>|Old water meters|Erroneous water meter consumption causing financial losses<br><br>|Recalibration of water meters Replacement of water meters<br><br>|
|Increasing demand is not being met by supply<br><br>|Depleted ground water source brought by continuous population growth<br><br>|Water interruptions causing health and economic damage<br><br>|Construction of additional Pumping Stations (Bancal PS-3 and Mabuhay)<br><br>|
|Occasional shut down of Pumping Station<br><br>|Due to power fluctuation and/or maintenance of generator sets<br><br>|Low water pressure in some areas|Close coordination with utility provider to report power failure and/or Periodic Preventive Maintenance of Generators Sets<br><br>|
|Lack of pressure on water system|Due to lack of elevated/groundwater tank(s)<br><br>|Lack of water supply in higher elevation areas<br><br>|Construction of Elevated/Groundwater Tank(s) to supply higher elevation areas<br><br>|
|Lack of Septage Facility to comply with the legal requirements on wastewater discharge<br><br>|No available Septage treatment plant in the area<br><br>|Possible contamination of ground water<br><br>|Construction of Septage Treatment Facility|
|Deferred water laboratory tests (Bacteriological Test and PhysicalChemical Tests) results<br><br>|No available in-house laboratory and lack of funding to outsource to labs<br><br>|Negative health for the communities<br><br>|Establishment of water testing facility/laboratory Providing funds ear marked for water testing<br><br>|
|Not operable Filtration System|Due to direct pumping, sand and other debris might be included in the distribution pipes<br><br>|Poor Water quality Lack of water pressure<br><br>|Procurement of Filtration System in Pumping Station<br><br>|
|Aging water pipelines|No available program/schedule for replacement of old pipelines<br><br>|Frequent leaks causing financial losses<br><br>|Program for replacement of old pipelines<br><br>|

![image 1549](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1549.png>)

|OBSERVED CONDITION/ ISSUES|EXPLANATION (CAUSES)|IMPLICATIONS WHEN UNRESOLVED<br><br>|STRATEGIES/ INTERVENTION/ POLICY OPTIONS<br><br>|
|---|---|---|---|

In Carmona, four (4) communication services facilities are available for transmitting information electronically. The Philippine Postal Corporation operates the municipal postal service located at the Department of Interior and Local Government (DILG) Building in Barangay Maduya. Snail mail or postal still plays an important role in the modern economy with bills, legal documents, letters, low cost package deliveries and announcements being coursed through the Philippine Postal Corporation.

In terms of internet connectivity, there are four (4) internet providers that currently exist in the municipality, including PLDT-SMART, which is also the major landline telephone provider in Carmona, along with Globe Telecom Inc., Converge ICT Solutions. Inc and the Southwoods Media Vision Inc.

PLDT is the major landline telephone provider in Carmona. Based on the results of 2017 CBMS Census Survey, a total of 22,745 households have either one (1) or more members using mobile phones. For telecommunication services, eleven (11) Telecom (mobile phone) relay antenna towers are available in Carmona. Seven of these are owned by Globe Telecom Inc.: three (3) in Brgy Maduya, one (1) in Cabilang-Baybay, and one (1) in Mabuhay and two (2) in Bancal. SMART Communications Inc relay antennas are also available in Bancal, Maduya, and Infocom in Barangay Mabuhay.

![image 1557](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1557.png>)

There is also an emergence for internet connectivity with a total of 8,438 household users as of 2017. Several households also used Two-way radios for their communication. A VHF repeater station tower located at Barangay Bancal increases the range of radio communication between local government agencies.

- D.2. TYPE OF PRINT MEDIA AVAILABLE About 90% of the total households in Carmona own television units. All television and radio stations from Manila have clear reception signals in

Carmona. Newspapers, magazines and books and other reading materials are also available.

- D.3. CELL SITE NETWORK The telecommunication facility in Carmona has a combined range of 16 sq. km at the minimum range for each cell site of 0.8 km radius or 2 sq. km

of area coverage. This means that to cover the entire 30.92 sq. km area of the municipality, 5 cell sites at 2 km regular interval should be constructed. Since the concentration of the sites were located in Maduya, Mabuhay, Cabilang Baybay and Bancal, it is quite logical to cover the areas of Lantic and the highlands between Bancal and Lantic to compensate for the lack of signal in these areas especially now that the residential areas of Lantic is fast expanding. #### CLIMATE AND DISASTER RISK ASSESSMENT (COMMUNICATION FACILITIES)

###### A. COMMUNICATION TOWER AFFECTED BY LANDSLIDE

Ten (10) Communication towers at various barangays are also vulnerable to landslide hazards, affecting 6,421.2 sq. m. of land. These towers are composed of Globe and Smart Telecom at Barangay Maduya, Cabilang Baybay, Mabuhay, and Bancal, with low landslide susceptibility and low-risk level. The affected barangays have low-risk categories since the landslide occurrence is occasional (every 11 to 30 years).

### Chapter 7: INSTITUTIONAL SECTOR

- A. THE LOCAL GOVERNMENT The Municipality of Carmona belongs to the 5th Congressional District of Cavite and among the 16 first class municipalities in the province. Under the provisions of the Local Government Code of 1991, the Municipal Mayor or the Local Chief Executive (LCE) heads the Municipal

Government of Carmona. The Mayor employs general supervision and control on all affairs relating to political and socio-economic concerns. He/she guarantees the effectiveness of programs, projects, services and activities of the local government by steering the workforce or all the units and departments under a common vision.

The Municipal Vice-Mayor acts as the presiding officer to the Sangguniang Bayan to which as a Body, their primary function is to enact ordinances, pass resolutions and appropriate funds intended for the general welfare of the municipality and its constituents. The former under circumstances of temporary or permanent vacancy in the Office of the Mayor, he/she is anticipated to assume the mandate of the Municipal Mayor.

![image 1583](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1583.png>)

Moreover, the SB has 22 standing committees. The chairmanship of each committee has been distributed among the 9 municipal councilors and vice-mayor.

The Local Chief Executive and members of the Sangguniang Bayan actively and regularly hold consultative meetings/dialogues in the barangay level and different sectors of the community in order to obtain the pulse, ire and satisfaction of the constituency. This serves as the basis for crafting necessary

![image 1588](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1588.png>)

legislation or intervention. Furthermore, the Municipality of Carmona is politically subdivided into 14 barangays. Each Barangay is headed by a Punong Barangay and aided by seven (7) Barangay Kagawad.

Aside from the Mayor, Vice-Mayor and Sangguniang Bayan Members, the LGU is headed by appointive municipal officials wherein 15 are mandatory positions as prescribed by the Local Government Code of 1991. However, in order to meet the expectations and demands of a growing community, additional managerial post (optional) were created (see Table 143):

As of 2019, the municipality of Carmona has 703 personnel composed of 468 male and 235 female employees. Additional seven (7) personnel were noted from the previous year’s 696 personnel. 184 are permanent employees, 411 are casual, 59 are under job order, 4 are coterminous, 12 are elected employees and 33 are contractual. The workforce is male dominated with 468 employees or 67% while 235 females make up only 33%. Of this manpower complement, the Personal Services Expenditure (PSE) is well within the 45% limitation.

![image 1601](<673810152-CLUP-Carmona-Volume-3-Final_images/imageFile1601.png>)

- B. INSTITUTIONAL CAPABILITY The numerous accomplishments of Carmona in recent years could be largely ascribed to the policy of participatory governance. The business

community, Non-Government Organizations (NGOs), People’s Organization (POs), National Government Agencies (NGAs), academe and the barangays as stakeholders play a vital part in outlining the future development directions of the LGU. Carmona’s vision realizes through strong, proactive and committed partnership of all sectors of the community.

- D. LOCAL SPECIAL BODIES Carmona created the local special bodies to comply with the requirements of the Local Government Code. One common feature of the local special

bodies is the participation of both the executive and legislative branches of the local government, and the membership of concerned local government offices or officials, national government agencies or their representatives, NGOs and private sector representatives. The Municipal Mayor is the Chairman of all Local Special Bodies in the municipality.

- E. LOCAL FISCAL MANAGEMENT In order to guarantee and sustain the provision of basic services and support to different programs/projects of the LGU, competence in operations

pertaining to real property tax appraisal, tax monitoring, zoning, business licensing, and other procedures are enhanced. The existing information technology support resulted to increased revenue collection and reduced transacting period for clients.

December to ensure fast tracked processing of permits. Downloadable forms are provided to cater busy clients. These best practices and innovations increased the municipality’s business competitiveness which in turn earned the adulation and regard of the Philippine Chamber of Commerce and Industry as the Most Business Friendly Municipality for 3 consecutive years.

The Municipal Budget Office has allocated P 881,434,217.85 budget for 2019. Of this amount P681, 381,776.40 (77.30%) were disbursed/ expended with a savings of 22.70% or P 200,052,441.45. MOOE showed biggest percentage of funding operation which is 48%, while Personnel Services showed 31% and Capital Outlay at 21%.

MOOE showed biggest percentage of funding operation which is 48%, followed by Personnel Services with 31% and Capital Outlay with 21%. The graph below also shows the corresponding expenditures for 2019. MOOE has the highest noted expenditures for this year with 46.53% or 317M; followed by PS with a total share of 34.89% or 237.7M and CO with 18.58% or 126.5M.

- F. LGU INCOME/REVENUE The municipality of Carmona generated a total income of 818,128,051.54 in 2019. This is higher by 6.57 % or 50, 427,060.97 compared to the

previous LGU income amounting to 767,700,990.57. Despite the decreasing trend in the percentage of increase, the LGU’s revenue still manifest a progressive amount every year.

- F.1. SOURCES OF REVENUE FOR THE LAST TWO YEARS Based on the previous years’ data, the major source of revenue is from local taxes (which include business & community taxes) which represents an

income share of 62% in 2018 and 56% in 2019. Carmona’s IRA increases by 10.46% from 180.4M in 2018 to 199.2M in 2019 which represents 24% income share. Other sources of revenue are from Real Property Tax, Permits & Licenses, Service Income and Other Income and Business Income which includes: cemetery operations, market, transportation & rent, interest &miscellaneous.
